# Work log

## 2026-08-21 — Foundation and live scan

- Updated `Agent.md` and the formal spec with an evidence boundary, three-state legality, strict scoring gate, and heuristic provenance.
- Implemented the Phase 0/1 Python foundation: config, logging, normalized models, standard parsers, scanner, CLI, fixtures, and tests.
- Added `baseline_0.1` heuristic registry values.
- Scanned the supplied Starsector installation read-only; reports/logs are under `generated/starsector_scan/`.
- Added relaxed JSON handling for comments and trailing commas after observing real mod compatibility issues.
- Identified the enabled-mod discovery and faction-patch parsing gaps. See `BUGS.md` and the generated malformed-file inventory.
- Created the generated malformed/nonstandard-file review queue from the live scan.
- Added syntax-only support for `#`, `//`, and block comments, trailing commas, and bare object keys; this improves parsing compatibility without interpreting mechanics.
- Began Phase 2 with deterministic entity indexing and explicit unresolved-reference reporting.
- Located and implemented `mods/enabled_mods.json` discovery. The enabled-mod-only scan now has 4,188 hulls, 4,711 weapons, 5,196 variants, 259 parser errors, and 18 explicit skipped items.
- Added `.ship` file ingestion for weapon-slot metadata, preserving source JSON and supplying mount ID/type/size/arc data needed by the future legality validator.
- Added the first evidence-bound legality validator: documented hull/mount/size/OP checks return `LEGAL`, `ILLEGAL`, or `NOT_DETERMINABLE`; it deliberately refuses to substitute quality heuristics for missing legality evidence.
- Added lexical `.wpn` spec ingestion for verified `id`, `type`, and `size` fields, allowing the validator to compare ordinary weapon and mount types without treating damage type as mount compatibility.
- Added `svg validate <variant_id>` and smoke-tested it on `apogee_Starting`. It returned `NOT_DETERMINABLE` with explicit non-weapon OP evidence limits, rather than an unsupported LEGAL claim. Report: `generated/starsector_validation/reports/validation_apogee_Starting.json`.
- Added SHA-256 source hashes and source-mod versions to normalized entities. Hashing is cached per scan and will support stale-artifact detection and cache invalidation.
- Added a persistent scan manifest under `generated/cache/`. It detects added, changed, and removed normalized source entities across scans and records the result in scan reports.
- Expanded hard OP validation using parsed hull size, built-in hullmods, hullmod OP tables, and fighter-wing OP values. Vents/capacitors remain explicitly indeterminate until sourced from documented configuration.
- Began Phase 4 with transparent, non-exclusive hull compatibility and weapon role tags. These classifiers are explicitly quality metadata and cannot alter legality outcomes.
- Began Phase 5 with a deterministic conservative weapon-only candidate builder and `svg generate`. It skips universal/undocumented mount semantics and validates every candidate before reporting. A live Lasher report was generated as `generated/starsector_generation/reports/candidate_lasher_LINE_ARTILLERY_svg.json`.
- Began Phase 6 with an explainable quality scorer. It has a strict LEGAL precondition and records range coherence, OP efficiency, role fit, and rationale without altering validation.
- Began Phase 8 export support. The compatibility-mod writer refuses non-LEGAL candidates and writes only generated `.variant`, `mod_info.json`, and README data outside game/mod source directories.
- Began Phase 7 mode support. Beginner, Guided, and Advanced are deterministic profile/access presets over the same generation, validation, and scoring pipeline.
- Added `svg export <hull_id>`, which runs the shared conservative generator and writes only a validated compatibility mod. Live smoke export: `generated/starsector_export/compatibility_mod/Starsector Auto Variants/`.
- Began Phase 9 golden regression coverage, locking baseline_0.1 deterministic candidate selection, component score, final score, and explanation text.
- Added `IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`, a requirement-by-requirement audit that explicitly records version 0.1 as incomplete and identifies the remaining formal-spec gaps.
- Added top-level trailing-comma and HJSON numeric-suffix normalization. A refreshed enabled-mod scan now parses 390 factions with only 18 parser errors; the review queue is refreshed under `generated/starsector_scan/reports/`.
- Corrected bare-value normalization to be string-aware and added single-quoted HJSON strings. The latest enabled-mod scan discovers 148 mods, parses 430 factions, and has 7 parse errors plus 3 explicitly skipped metadata files.
- Added a Windows-1252 CSV fallback for legacy mods. The latest scan now has 6 parser errors and 3 skipped metadata files; all remain in the generated review queue.
- Isolated `.ship` parse failures per hull row so one malformed ship no longer aborts its entire `ship_data.csv`. The live scan now ingests 4,427 hulls and reports the exact malformed `.ship` paths.
- Added safe escaping for legacy control characters inside strings. The live scan now ingests 4,430 hulls and has only 3 parser errors plus 3 explicitly skipped metadata files.
- Added semicolon-delimiter normalization outside strings. The latest enabled-mod scan has zero parser errors, 4,430 hulls, 5,606 variants, and 431 factions; three malformed mod metadata files remain explicitly skipped and queued.
- Normalized nested faction known-hull, weapon, fighter, and hullmod lists, providing direct source evidence for upcoming faction restriction and doctrine analysis.
- Added an evidence-only faction doctrine analyzer that summarizes existing-variant weapon ranges and repeated hullmods while explicitly avoiding legality or optimality claims.
- Added strict faction equipment filtering to generation. `STRICT_FACTION` now requires an indexed faction and admits only its explicitly parsed known weapons; it never infers access from names or source folders.
- Added `svg doctrine <faction_id> [--source-mod ...]`. Ambiguous faction overrides require explicit source selection; a live core Hegemony report is stored in `generated/starsector_doctrine/reports/doctrine_hegemony.json`.
- Added `svg analyze-variant <variant_id>`, which serializes the legality assessment and only includes quality results when the same candidate is LEGAL.
- Added deterministic mod dependency auditing to the registry; absent declared dependencies are reported explicitly instead of being assumed available.
- Added registry diagnostics to scan reports: unresolved entity references and missing declared dependencies now persist with the same scan audit trail.
- Compatibility-mod export now writes a generation manifest with generator identity, candidate ID, and source hull/weapon hashes for stale-output inspection.
- Added `svg check-export <generation_manifest.json>`, which performs a fresh read-only scan and reports whether a generated export's recorded hull/weapon source hashes are current. Missing or malformed provenance is deliberately stale rather than silently accepted.
- Observed and resolved SVG-006: dependency declarations in the supplied mods can be objects with explicit `id`, display-name, and version fields. The scanner now extracts only the declared ID for presence auditing, retains no version compatibility inference, and queues any entry without an explicit string ID.
- Verified the new export-audit flow against the supplied installation: the pre-manifest workspace export was correctly reported stale because its provenance file was absent; regenerated `lasher_LINE_ARTILLERY_svg.variant` is `CURRENT`. Evidence: `generated/starsector_export/reports/export_staleness.json`. Full suite: 36 tests passing.
- Made `FACTION_PLUS` distinct from unrestricted generation for weapons: when a resolved faction provides explicit known-weapon evidence, native compatible weapons sort first; a documented-compatible fallback remains eligible. Without a resolved faction, the generated report marks faction preference evidence as unavailable rather than claiming it was applied.
- Added a five-profile, deterministic quality catalog (`LINE_BRAWLER`, `LINE_ARTILLERY`, `FAST_STRIKE`, `MISSILE_SUPPORT`, and `CARRIER_SUPPORT`) and `svg list-profiles`. Missile and carrier profiles state their current weapon-only limits instead of implying unsupported fitting behavior. Full suite: 38 tests passing.
- Extended `baseline_0.1` with immutable per-value rationale, units, affected profiles/modes, and kind metadata. Export manifests now record both the selected heuristic-set identifier and resolved values; missing fields make the export stale during audit.
- Extended export provenance with the generation timestamp, selected profile, and faction mode; these are metadata only and never alter legality or quality evaluation.
- Regression verification after the profile, heuristic-registry, and export-provenance updates: 39 tests passing.
- Added strict Advanced-mode request support for implemented weapon restrictions: allow/deny lists, mount locks, and explicit empty mounts. Unsupported options are rejected instead of silently ignored. Live sample report: `generated/starsector_advanced/reports/candidate_lasher_LINE_ARTILLERY_svg.json`; initial run surfaced and resolved SVG-007.
- Corrected the README to reflect the implemented scan/validation/generation/scoring/modes/export commands and to point readers to the authoritative incomplete-status audit. Regression verification after SVG-007: 43 tests passing.
- Added fail-closed output collision protection: identical exports are idempotent, but differing pre-existing `.variant`, mod metadata, README, or per-variant manifests are never overwritten. Export manifests are now named `generation_manifest_<variant_id>.json` so multiple generated variants can coexist in one compatibility mod.
- Added Phase 2 query APIs and `svg query`: explicitly filtered weapons, variants by hull, and faction equipment reports. Queries intentionally omit duplicate IDs and require a source mod when a faction override is ambiguous.
- Live query smoke test: `svg query weapons --size MEDIUM --mount-type BALLISTIC` produced `generated/starsector_queries/reports/query_weapons.json` from a fresh read-only scan. The fixture query test was corrected to use its declared faction ID (`fixture`) after the registry properly rejected the undeclared test ID.
- Regression verification after collision-safe export and registry-query additions: 46 tests passing.
- Refreshed the malformed/nonstandard review with the current module entry point: 4,430 hulls, 4,994 weapons, 5,606 variants, 431 factions, and zero parser errors. Only three enabled-but-unavailable IDs remain. Researched and documented them in `MOD_REVIEW.md`; all are gameplay mods, not utilities, and no source/launcher files were changed.
- Normalized documented `.ship` launch-bay slots and built-in fighter-wing declarations as hull evidence for carrier classification. No fighter-bay capacity legality rule was added because its semantics have not been independently established.
- Strengthened legality reference validation: missing hullmods and fighter wings are now explicit `ILLEGAL` findings rather than being blurred into missing OP uncertainty.
- Regression verification after normalized carrier evidence and reference-validation tightening: 47 tests passing.
- Added descriptive fighter and hullmod classifiers. They expose only parsed source role/properties and deliberately do not make gameplay-synergy or legality claims.
- Regression verification after fighter/hullmod classifier coverage: 48 tests passing.
- Normalized structured mod-version provenance to deterministic JSON text, preserving values without assigning version-comparison semantics.
- Added bounded deterministic multi-candidate generation: a conservative baseline plus one next-ranked documented weapon alternative per mount, capped by `--max-candidates` (1-20). Every alternative retains its own legality result; CLI ranking is legal-only and cannot promote an `ILLEGAL` or `NOT_DETERMINABLE` candidate.
- Observed and resolved SVG-008 during the refactor: targeted tests caught a missing internal hull/profile lookup before release. Added regression coverage for distinct deterministic alternatives and invalid bounds. Full suite: 51 tests passing.
- Generated a fresh live bounded-search report for `lasher` / `LINE_ARTILLERY` from the supplied installation: 5 independently `LEGAL` alternatives at `generated/starsector_generation_current/reports/candidates_lasher_LINE_ARTILLERY.json`.
- Added source-provenance dependency records to export manifests and fail-closed staleness validation. They record explicit source-mod labels/versions for referenced hulls and weapons but make no claim about game dependency or load-order semantics. Verification is pending: the environment rejected the attempted export-test command because its usage limit was reached.

- 2026-08-22: Drafted a five-tier gap-closing plan (legality completeness, flux scoring, doctrine/classification, generation breadth, infrastructure) against `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`'s material gaps and `Forge formal spec.txt`, plus a GUI blueprint explicitly gated behind the Definition-of-Done per `Agent.md`. Published as an Artifact during planning; the durable in-repo record is `docs/ROADMAP.md`.
- 2026-08-22: Before implementing, verified real field names against `generated/starsector_scan_current/reports/scan_summary.json` (a prior live scan's full entity dump, including `raw` fields the typed models didn't capture) rather than guessing them from memory: confirmed `max flux`/`flux dissipation`/`shield upkeep` hull CSV columns, `energy/shot`/`energy/second` weapon CSV columns, and `ship_data.builtInWeapons` (`{mount_id: weapon_id}`) for `weaponSlots` entries with `"type": "BUILT_IN"`. This is why Tier 2 and Tier 1.4 could be implemented directly while Tier 1.1 (vent/cap OP cost table) and Tier 1.2 (hullmod incompatibility) could not -- neither's real values were present in that scan, and Agent.md forbids inferring undocumented game behavior.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented Tier 1.3 (fighter-bay capacity, fail-closed to `NOT_DETERMINABLE` when a hull has no parsed `.ship` data) and Tier 1.4 (built-in-weapon-mount preservation) in `validation/legality.py`, extending `Hull`/`Weapon` and their parsers accordingly.
- 2026-08-22: Added the `adapters/` package (`adapters/vanilla/INCOMPATIBLE_HULLMOD_PAIRS`) per Agent.md's adapter-layer requirement for undocumented custom mechanics, wired into a new `HULLMOD_INCOMPATIBLE` legality check. Shipped deliberately empty (SVG-010): populating an entry requires a citable source, and none was verified during this pass.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented Tier 2 (flux scoring) per the formal spec's `sustained_flux_load`/`dissipation_ratio` formula, and Tier 3.1 (`doctrine_match`, wired into scoring only when a resolved faction is supplied). Both fail closed: missing flux data or faction evidence removes that component from the weighted score rather than defaulting it to a penalizing zero. Discovered Tier 3.2 (hull role classification) was already implemented (`analysis/classification.py::classify_hull`) prior to this pass; the original gap audit had mis-flagged it.
- 2026-08-22: Added `baseline_0.2` to the heuristic registry (`core/heuristics.py`) as a new, additive identifier -- `baseline_0.1` was left byte-for-byte unchanged and remains pinned by the golden regression test. `scoring/candidate_score.py::score_candidate` branches on whether the supplied heuristic_set has the new `weight_*` keys; if not, it reproduces the original three-component formula exactly. Bumped the default `heuristic_set` to `baseline_0.2` in `core/config.py::AppConfig` and `profiles/modes.py::resolve_mode` (all three `UserMode` presets), updating `tests/test_config_and_heuristics.py`'s expectation deliberately to match.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented Tier 4's Guided-mode "meaningful choice": `--flux-mode` (`SAFE`/`BALANCED`/`AGGRESSIVE`) on `svg generate` and `svg analyze-variant`, defaulted per `UserMode` (`ModeDefaults.flux_mode`), reusing the existing generation/scoring engine with no duplicated logic. Left the hullmod selector, vent/cap allocator, and weapon-group builder unimplemented and documented as blocked/undesigned in `docs/ROADMAP.md`, since the first two depend on Tier 1.1/1.2's unresolved data and the third has no decided grouping rule.
- 2026-08-22: Added `.github/workflows/tests.yml` (Tier 5): runs the unittest suite on Python 3.11/3.12 and a `python -m build` check on push/PR. Left the SQLite cache deferred per the formal spec's own "once the data model stabilizes" condition, which this pass's Tier 1.4/Tier 2 model changes do not yet satisfy.
- 2026-08-22: Extended `tests/fixtures/vanilla_mod` (hull `.ship`/CSV, weapon CSV) with a `BUILT_IN`-type mount and flux columns so the new parser/legality paths have fixture coverage, not just live-scan verification. Full suite: 65 tests passing, including an unmodified `test_regression.py` golden test verifying `baseline_0.1` output is unaffected by the scoring rework. Did not run a live CLI smoke test against a real installation (none was available in this environment); recommended before this work is considered fully verified, consistent with prior phases' practice of a live smoke test alongside unit coverage.
- 2026-08-22: Explicitly did not implement the GUI blueprint from the same planning pass. `Agent.md` bars a GUI before the core engine is ready, and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md` shows v0.1 is not complete; the blueprint is preserved (published Artifact plus `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s summary) for whenever that gate is met and sign-off is given.
- 2026-08-22 (continued, given live install + web access): Resolved Tier 1.1 for real. `data/config/settings.json` on the live installation gives `fluxPerCapacitor:200`/`dissipationPerVent:10` (per-unit effect, now parsed data, not a citation); OP cost and hull-size maximum are engine-hardcoded and were verified via https://starsector.wiki.gg/wiki/Flux (1 OP per unit; Frigate 10/Destroyer 20/Cruiser 30/Capital 50), cross-checked against the installed settings.json's matching per-unit values before being trusted. `adapters/vanilla/FLUX_UNIT_COST` now backs real `FLUX_VENTS_EXCEED_HULL_MAXIMUM`/`FLUX_CAPACITORS_EXCEED_HULL_MAXIMUM` legality checks.
- 2026-08-22: Re-researched Tier 1.2 (hullmod incompatibility) via web search; found no evidence vanilla has a *pairwise* exclusivity mechanic at all (two independent wiki queries). Found the real mechanic instead: `settings.json`'s `maxLogisticsHullmods:2`, matching https://starsector.wiki.gg/wiki/Hullmod's "limited to a maximum of 2 logistics hullmods." Implemented `MAX_LOGISTICS_HULLMODS`/`LOGISTICS_HULLMOD_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, counting only hullmods whose parsed `uiTags` CSV column positively contains "Logistics" (never assuming non-logistics from missing/different tags). SVG-010 updated accordingly; the pairwise table stays empty by design, not by oversight.
- 2026-08-22: Live-stress-tested all 441 real core (vanilla) variants against every new legality rule. Caught and fixed a real bug: `FIGHTER_BAY_CAPACITY_EXCEEDED` false-positived on 15 real variants (e.g. `condor_Attack`) because `len(hull.launch_bay_slots)` undercounts hulls where one `LAUNCH_BAY`-type `weaponSlot` entry's multi-pair `locations` array represents several physical bays (verified: Condor has one such slot but a CSV `"fighter bays"` count of 2). Added `Hull.fighter_bays` (parsed from that CSV column) as the authoritative capacity and re-ran the same 441-variant stress test: zero false positives on every rule (fighter bays, built-in weapons, vent/cap limits, logistics-hullmod cap) after the fix. This is the reason to always live-verify a new legality rule against real data before trusting it, not just hand-built fixtures.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented Tier 4's vent/cap allocator (`generation/vent_cap.py`, `allocate_vents_and_capacitors`), now unblocked by the Tier 1.1 resolution above. Spends remaining OP on vents toward the profile's flux target, then capacitors with what's left, capped by the same documented hull-size maximums the legality check enforces -- so a generated candidate can never fail its own legality check for vent/cap reasons. Declines to allocate anything (returns zeros) when flux data is incomplete or the source mod has no documented flux-unit cost, matching the legacy weapon-only behavior exactly for every existing test fixture. Wired into `generate_conservative_candidate`/`generate_candidate_alternatives` via a new `flux_mode` parameter; `svg generate` now passes its resolved `--flux-mode` through to generation, not just scoring.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented weapon groups (the previously-undesigned part of Tier 4) by deciding a grounded default rather than leaving it open indefinitely: `output/writer.py::_weapon_groups` splits PD-tagged weapons (via the already-tested `classify_weapon` "PD" tag) into an AUTOFIRE group and everything else into a LINKED group, since PD is conventionally autofire in Starsector and a single LINKED group would leave the player to fire it manually. Also fixed a related gap while in this function: `write_variant` was silently dropping `flux_vents`/`flux_capacitors` from exported `.variant` files (never written at all); now included when allocated, omitted (not written as `null`) when not, since a `null` value's effect on actual game-load is unverified.
- 2026-08-22: Extended `svg analyze-variant` with `--faction-id`/`--source-mod` (same disambiguation pattern as `svg doctrine`) so `faction_doctrine_match` can surface there too, not just in `svg generate`. Live-verified against a real LEGAL variant (`apogee_Balanced`) and a real faction (`tritachyon`, disambiguated from 20+ mod-duplicated `tritachyon` ids): `faction_doctrine_match: 0.6` appeared correctly in the report alongside an honest "flux_sustainability is not evaluated" note for weapons lacking flux data.
- 2026-08-22: Discovered and reconciled a second planning-doc drop: `Starsector-Variant-Generator-Planning-Pack/` (`AGENTS.md`, `FORMAL_SPECIFICATION.md` v0.2, `GUI.md`, `DATA_SCHEMA.md`, `HEURISTICS.md`, `TEST_PLAN.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `README.md`, `config/`). The same content had already landed at repo root minutes earlier with **scrambled filenames** (a bug in whatever produced it, confirmed by diffing against the pack); root files were corrected to match the pack. Full reconciliation notes -- including that `FORMAL_SPECIFICATION.md` v0.2 trims some v0.1 safety-rule elaboration without reversing it (`AGENTS.md` remains higher-precedence and restates the same rules), that `GUI.md`'s PySide6/Qt Ship-Fitting-Canvas design supersedes this session's earlier web-based GUI blueprint Artifact, and that the Manual Override Layer (`FORMAL_SPECIFICATION.md` section 46) is a real, unimplemented, well-specified feature -- are in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s "Doc set integration" section, not duplicated here. Project's eventual name confirmed as **VoidSmith**.
- 2026-08-22: Verified real field/data findings from this session don't just hold for the vanilla scan: spot-checked the full legality+scoring pipeline against a randomly selected installed mod (`missingsuperships`, 55 variants) -- zero exceptions, and every failure/uncertainty code produced traced to an already-documented gap (`MOUNT_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `VENT_CAP_OP_UNKNOWN`, `FIGHTER_BAY_CAPACITY_UNKNOWN`), with none of this session's new checks false-positiving. Full local suite: 78 tests passing.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented the hullmod selector (`generation/hullmods.py::select_hullmods`), the last unimplemented piece of Tier 4. Rather than fabricate per-profile "this hullmod suits this role" judgments, it reuses the exact evidence pattern weapon selection already uses: a faction's real `known_hullmods` as preferred/allowed, cheapest-OP-first otherwise. First implementation had no cap on hullmod count and produced a 20-hullmod frigate on live data -- checked real evidence instead of guessing a fix (324 live core variants with any hullmods: median/mean ~2, 88% have 1-3) and capped `max_hullmods` at 2 accordingly. Full 85-test suite passing before the live stress test below.
- 2026-08-22: Stress-tested the complete weapons+hullmods+vents/caps generator across all 158 real core combat hulls x 3 profiles (474 candidates). First run: 458/474 LEGAL, 16 ILLEGAL -- all 16 traced to `BUILT_IN_WEAPON_OVERRIDDEN` (53 individual findings across those 16) plus 3 pre-existing `MOUNT_TYPE_MISMATCH`. Root-caused SVG-012: some real vanilla weapons declare `mountType` `"BUILT_IN"` themselves, so the generator's mount loop (which only ever excluded `UNIVERSAL`-type mounts, never `BUILT_IN`-type ones) would occasionally consider them "eligible" for a hull's fixed built-in mount regardless of which specific weapon that hull actually hardwires there. Tier 1.4's legality check caught every instance correctly -- no illegal export was ever possible -- but the generator shouldn't have proposed those candidates at all. Fixed in `generation/candidate.py::_build_candidate` (a `hull.built_in_weapons` mount is now always left for the game to auto-fill); added a regression test; re-ran the same 474-candidate stress test: 471/474 (99.4%) LEGAL, zero `BUILT_IN_WEAPON_OVERRIDDEN`, remaining 3 unchanged (pre-existing Universal-mount gap). Full suite: 86 tests passing.
- 2026-08-22: Random-mod spot-check #2 per ongoing practice: `assortment_of_things`, 26 of its own combat hulls generated with its own (non-vanilla-restricted) weapons/hullmods -- 26/26 LEGAL, zero exceptions.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented the fighter-wing selector (`generation/fighters.py::select_fighter_wings`), unblocked by the Tier 1.3 `Hull.fighter_bays` fix -- `CARRIER_SUPPORT` was a weapon-only fallback until now. Unlike hullmods, fighter wings have a real physical capacity (the same `fighter_bays` field the legality check already uses), so no invented cap was needed: filling every bay subject to OP is the natural, evidence-grounded conservative behavior. `preferred_wing_ids` reuses the faction-`known_fighters` evidence pattern, wired into `svg generate` alongside weapons/hullmods. Stress-tested against all 19 real carrier-capable core hulls: 18/19 LEGAL, 0 exceptions; verified `STRICT_FACTION`-style filtering restricts selection to a faction's real known fighters. Full suite: 91 tests passing.
- 2026-08-22: Expanded the profile catalog from 5 to 7 (user suggested adding classes to better fit "carrier or tank" roles): `TANK` (hullmod selection prefers the hull's real `uiTags` "Defenses" category) and `PD_ESCORT` (weapon selection prefers `classify_weapon`'s already-tested "PD" tag). Added `RoleProfile.hullmod_priority_tag` (consumed by `select_hullmods` as a secondary sort key after faction-preferred hullmods, before cost) and a `weapon_priority` value `"PD_FIRST"` (consumed the same way in `_build_candidate`'s weapon sort). Both are documented-category preferences derived from evidence already parsed and tested elsewhere in the codebase, not new per-item judgments. `CARRIER_SUPPORT` also given `hullmod_priority_tag="Fighters"`. Verified live: `TANK` on `lasher` selected `armoredweapons`/`assault_package` (both real "Defenses"-tagged hullmods) where `LINE_BRAWLER` on the same hull selected different, non-defense-tagged ones. All 7 profiles then stress-tested together across all 158 real core combat hulls (1,106 candidates): 157/158 (99.4%) LEGAL for every profile, 0 exceptions. `docs/profiles.md` and `tests/test_profiles.py` updated. Full suite: 93 tests passing.
- 2026-08-22: Random-mod spot-check #3: `exshippack`, 20 hulls x all 7 profiles (140 candidates) -- 140/140 LEGAL, zero exceptions.
- 2026-08-22: Resolved SVG-013, the largest single false-positive source found this session: both `validate_variant` and `_build_candidate` only ever did an exact string match between a mount's `type` and a weapon's `mount_type`, correct for `BALLISTIC`/`ENERGY`/`MISSILE` but wrong for `HYBRID`/`COMPOSITE`/`SYNERGY`/`UNIVERSAL` mounts, which accept documented combinations. Verified via three independent, mutually corroborating sources (two starsector.wiki.gg pages, plus an empirical scan of every weapon-to-mount assignment across all 431 real developer-authored core variants -- e.g. 59 `BALLISTIC`-type and 56 `ENERGY`-type weapons in `HYBRID`-type mounts, matching the wiki exactly, 0 exceptions) before encoding it as `core/mount_compatibility.py::MOUNT_TYPE_COMPATIBILITY`. Fixing it surfaced a second, related pre-existing bug: a `BUILT_IN` mount's own `type` field was being checked against the same matrix, which would have failed even the hull's own correct built-in weapon (whose real `mount_type` is never `"BUILT_IN"`) -- fixed by skipping the generic size/type checks entirely for mounts present in `hull.built_in_weapons`, since `BUILT_IN_WEAPON_OVERRIDDEN` already governs those exclusively. Two pre-existing test fixtures (which had used an artificial `mount_type="BUILT_IN"` weapon as a convenience shortcut) failed and were fixed by restructuring, not by weakening assertions. Re-verified against all 441 real core variants: `MOUNT_TYPE_MISMATCH` dropped from 299 to 4 (98.7% reduction), `LEGAL` count rose from 162 to 293 (+81%). The remaining 4 were individually inspected and confirmed to all be `hull_size: FIGHTER` wings -- a genuinely separate, still-undocumented mount-semantics area, correctly left alone. Full suite: 99 tests passing (6 new: `tests/test_mount_compatibility.py` plus 2 integration tests in `tests/test_legality.py`).
- 2026-08-22: Implemented richer bounded search: `generate_candidate_alternatives` gained `search_depth` (default 1 -- verified byte-identical to every existing caller's prior output, including the golden regression test), exploring rank 2, 3, ... per mount breadth-first across ranks rather than just one alternate rank. Still one mount changed at a time, so candidate count stays linear (mount count x depth), never combinatorial, per Agent.md's rule against brute-force generation. Wired into `svg generate` as `--search-depth` (1-10), recorded in the report's `bounded_search` block. Live-verified on `lasher` at depth 3 (15/15 LEGAL, all distinct loadouts) and stress-tested at depth 4 across 60 real core hulls (788/788 LEGAL, 0 exceptions). Full suite: 102 tests passing.
- 2026-08-22: Implemented the weapons scope of the Manual Override Layer (`FORMAL_SPECIFICATION.md` section 46, surfaced by the earlier planning-pack integration): `core/overrides.py` (`load_overrides`, `apply_role_tag_override`). The spec names the override files but doesn't pin an exact schema, so picked one explicitly: a JSON object keyed by entity id, additive-only (unions into the classifier's own tags, never removes/replaces evidence), structurally unable to touch legality (the module isn't imported by `validation/legality.py` at all). Discovered while scoping this that `classify_weapon`/`classify_hull`/etc. had zero CLI surface before now -- only consumed internally for PD-priority sorting and PD-vs-linked weapon groups -- so wired `role_tags`/`range_band`/`role_tags_overridden` into `svg query weapons` (`--overrides-dir`, default `config/overrides`) as the first user-visible surface, rather than building an override system with nothing to visibly affect. Live-verified end to end: overriding `lightmg` added a test tag to its real classifier tags (`KINETIC_PRESSURE`, `PD`), correctly flagged as overridden, with every other weapon in the same query correctly flagged as not. Hulls/fighters/hullmods/factions scope and generation/export wiring (letting an override actually change `PD_ESCORT` selection or weapon-group export, not just query output) are explicitly left open -- see `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s dedicated section. Full suite: 108 tests passing (6 new: `tests/test_overrides.py`).

Found the hull CSV's own `hints` column carries real, explicit role evidence (`CIVILIAN`/`FREIGHTER`/`TANKER`/`LINER`/`CARRIER`/`COMBAT`/`TRANSPORT`, verified across all 211 live core hulls) -- far more reliable than the numeric approach: checked whether cargo-capacity ratios could discriminate civilian from combat hulls for the hulls without explicit hints, and found they couldn't (civilian-hinted median cargo 95 vs combat-hinted median 100, nearly identical), so deliberately did NOT build a numeric threshold classifier on that weak evidence. Added `Hull.cargo_capacity`/`fuel_capacity`/`crew_min`/`crew_max`/`supplies_per_month`/`max_burn`/`hull_hints`, `analysis/classification.py::classify_civilian_role` (evidence-only tags, filtering out non-role structural markers like `MODULE`/`STATION`), `Registry.hulls_matching`, and `svg query hulls --civilian-only` (classification had zero CLI surface before this session's weapons-override work, and hulls had none at all until now). Live-verified: exactly 17 real core civilian hulls returned, matching the hint count exactly, with sensible per-hull numbers (`dram`/TANKER: fuel=300 vs cargo=15; `atlas`/FREIGHTER: cargo=2000). - 2026-08-22: Added an OP-efficiency metric to the Hullmod Effect Engine's civilian slice, per `HULLMODS_CIVILIAN_AND_REFIT.md` section 9's own suggested `cargo / OP_spent_on_logistics` formula -- deliberately used instead of a fabricated 0-100 "civilian quality score" for the same reason survivability scoring was rejected earlier this session: no defensible absolute normalization scale exists, while a per-OP ratio is self-normalizing and reflects the fitting choice rather than the hull's fixed base stats. `analysis/civilian.py::AppliedLogisticsEffect` gained `op_cost`/`efficiency` fields (looked up from the same real `Hullmod.op_cost_by_hull_size` data the legality/generation pipeline already uses); `compute_derived_civilian_stats` gained a required `registry` parameter to resolve it, and `applied_effect_hullmod_ids` became a computed property over the new `applied_effects` tuple. `efficiency` is `None` (not a fabricated 0) whenever the OP cost isn't resolvable, matching the existing "absence of verified data is never treated as absence of effect" pattern. - 2026-08-22: Fixed SVG-014, the scanner gap `faction-capability`'s live stress test surfaced. Added `.skin` ingestion: `parsers/entities.py::hull_from_skin` materializes a derived `Hull` from a base hull plus a skin's explicit override lists (mount remove/change, built-in-mods/weapons/wings remove-then-add, hint remove/add, scalar OP/name/fighter-bay/supplies overrides), and preserves every skin field this project doesn't yet model as a typed attribute in `raw` rather than guessing at it. `core/scanner.py` discovers `data/hulls/skins/*.skin` per mod but defers resolution to a single pass after every mod has been scanned (a skin can reference a base hull from a different mod), and never guesses at an unresolved or ambiguous `baseHullId` -- reports a warning instead. Also fixed a related, freshly-discovered real gap in the same pass: 20 of the live install's own core `.skin` files use invalid-JSON bare-leading-decimal numbers (`.7` instead of `0.7`); extended `parsers/common.py::_relaxed_json`'s existing HJSON-tolerance normalization to handle it, following the exact same precedent as the existing numeric-suffix (`1f`) normalization. 11 new tests (7 unit tests for `hull_from_skin` covering scalar overrides/mount remove-and-change/built-in-mod and -weapon remove-then-add/hint remove-and-add/missing-skin-id/raw-preservation, 2 scanner-integration tests using new real-shaped fixture `.skin` files, 1 leading-decimal-JSON test, plus the pre-existing hull-count assertion updated). Full suite: 135 tests passing. Live stress-tested against the full real install: hull count rose from 4,430 to 4,736 (+306 real skinned hulls, e.g. every core `_d`/`_xiv`/faction-variant ship), skin parse errors dropped from 29 (before the leading-decimal fix) to 5 (all genuinely malformed source files with mismatched braces, correctly left as errors), 2 real data-quality warnings correctly surfaced rather than guessed through. Re-ran `faction-capability hegemony`: `unresolved_known_hull_ids` dropped from 9 to 0, and `MISSILE_SUPPORT`'s best-known-hull evidence changed from `enforcer` (0.44) to the now-resolved `kite_hegemony` (0.67) -- a real accuracy improvement, not just a count fix.

Live-verified against the same real `phantom_Elite` variant used to validate the original engine (it has both `auxiliary_fuel_tanks` and `efficiency_overhaul`): `efficiency_overhaul` now correctly resolves (base supplies 10 -> 8, base min crew 5 -> 4, exact 20%), where before this fix it was the one entry in `unverified_hullmod_ids`. - 2026-08-22: Researched the remaining 4 unresearched real LOGISTICS hullmods (`hiressensors`, `insulatedengine`, `solar_shielding`, `surveying_equipment`) and found each genuinely NOT modelable in this project's current per-ship scope -- not an oversight, a deliberate exclusion for a specific reason each: `hiressensors`'s real effect is a whole-fleet campaign-map sensor bonus (explicitly deferred scope); `insulatedengine`'s three effects each need either an unmodeled "engine" sub-stat, an unmodeled campaign-detection stat, or would mean parsing `hitpoints` (a real CSV column) for a tenth of one hullmod's value with no other consumer; `solar_shielding`'s energy-damage-reduction effect would mean modeling live combat damage math, which the formal spec explicitly rules out ("combat simulation" is permanently out of scope); `surveying_equipment` reduces a campaign-action's resource cost, not a ship stat at all. Documented all 4 findings directly in `adapters/vanilla/__init__.py` (with OP costs cross-checked against live install regardless, so the research isn't wasted) so a future session doesn't re-investigate from scratch. The 5th, `militarized_subsystems`, turned out to be fully modelable: +1 max burn (flat) and +100% (doubled) minimum crew required -- both stats this project already tracks. Implementing it required a real, evidence-driven schema fix: `HullmodLogisticsEffect` lookups were keyed one-effect-per-hullmod-id, but this hullmod needs two (one per stat); changed `compute_derived_civilian_stats` to group effects by hullmod id into a list and apply every matching entry. 6 new tests (2 civilian.py, matching the militarized_subsystems two-effect case and its partial-verification edge case). Full suite: 150 tests passing. Live-verified against the real `venture_Balanced` variant: max_burn 7 -> 8 (exact +1) and crew_min 80 -> 160 (exact doubling). Stress-tested `analyze_variant` across all 5,606 real variants in the live install: 0 exceptions, `militarized_subsystems` fired correctly on all 3 real variants that use it. LOGISTICS hullmod coverage is now 6 of 10 real ones (the other 4 deliberately excluded, not gaps).

- 2026-08-22: Implemented the Gap Recommendation Engine, v1 (native-only), per a design the project owner supplied and asked me to assess for doability first. Assessment: the full design (native/retrofit/acquisition search, retrofit cost, role distortion, confidence propagation, knowledge-pack doctrine bias) is well-conceived and consistent with the newly-synced `FACTION_KNOWLEDGE_PACKS.md`, but structurally depends on 3 unstarted phases (7 Knowledge Packs, 8 Equipment Access/Adaptive Autofit, 9 Refit Assistant) -- retrofit-cost specifically has nothing to measure against without a Refit Assistant to diff. Recommended (and the project owner chose): write the full spec now as `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md`, but scope actual code to the one leg that doesn't need those phases -- native search, which only needs the already-partially-built Faction Capability Analyzer. Added `core/heuristics.py` gap-severity/count thresholds (`gap_strong_threshold`/`gap_adequate_threshold`/`gap_weak_threshold`/`gap_recommendation_count`, `baseline_0.2`, first-pass values per Agent.md's versioned-heuristic rule) and `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` (`detect_capability_gaps`, `recommend_native_solutions`), wired into `api.py::run_gap_recommendations` and `svg recommend <faction_id>`. Writing this engine's own tests caught a real design bug before any release: the original formula (`capability_gain = candidate_score - faction_existing_coverage`, mirrored from the supplied design's section 6) is structurally always <= 0 for native search specifically, because `faction_existing_coverage` is itself defined as the best score *within the same native hull set* being ranked -- no native hull can ever beat a baseline derived from its own set's maximum. Traced the root cause rather than adjusting the test to fit, fixed both the code (rank by raw `capability_score`, not a gain) and the design doc (with the bug and fix documented inline, not silently corrected). 9 new tests (`tests/test_gap_recommendation.py`). Full suite: 159 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: Hegemony (well-rounded) shows 0 gaps; Pirates shows a real, sensible `CARRIER` gap with zero native solutions (`unaddressed_gaps`) plus `BATTLE_CARRIER`/`LINE_ARTILLERY` weak spots with ranked native alternatives (`atlas2`, `manticore_pirates`) -- matching real Starsector faction knowledge (pirates have no dedicated carrier hulls). Stress-tested across all 431 real factions in the live install: 0 exceptions. Root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 10 status will be updated to reflect this (native leg only; retrofit/acquisition/explainability remain NOT_STARTED, correctly gated on Phases 7-9).

- 2026-08-22: Started root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 8 (Equipment Access + Adaptive Autofit, new in the v0.5 pack refresh) with its most tractable first slice: equipment affinity classification (`EQUIPMENT_ACCESS_AND_AUTOFIT.md` sections 2/5/6/8), without the `ADAPTIVE`/`STARSECTOR_STYLE` substitution engine those sections also describe, which needs a Refit Assistant (Phase 9, unstarted) to have anything to substitute within. `analysis/equipment_affinity.py::classify_equipment_affinity` classifies a weapon/fighter/hullmod's real faction-ownership evidence purely from already-parsed `known_weapons`/`known_fighters`/`known_hullmods` lists across every real faction (including ambiguous-id duplicates) -- `NATIVE` (requesting faction owns it), `COMMON` (4+ factions, a judgment-call threshold, not a documented mechanic), `FOREIGN` (fewer factions, none the requester), or `UNALIGNED` (no faction references it at all). Deliberately does not produce `APPROVED`/`RESTRICTED`/`UNKNOWN` -- those need knowledge-pack or explicit-override evidence this project doesn't have yet (Phase 7) -- and never infers affinity from `source_mod_id` alone, per the spec's explicit warning. Wired into `svg query weapons` (`faction_affinity`/`owning_faction_ids` fields, using the command's existing `--faction-id` as "requesting faction" context). 8 new tests. Full suite: 167 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: querying all 3,193 real weapons with `--faction-id hegemony` gave a plausible distribution (2,822 UNALIGNED, 270 FOREIGN, 59 NATIVE, 42 COMMON), correctly showing NATIVE winning over COMMON when the requesting faction is one of several owners (e.g. `A_S-F_bramble`, owned by all 9 core factions including Hegemony); running without a faction produced 0 NATIVE and a slightly larger COMMON bucket (97), as expected.

- 2026-08-22: Implemented root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 7 (Faction Knowledge Pack Framework), first slice, against a concrete schema/example/loader design the project owner supplied directly (a three-artifact split: machine-enforced JSON Schema, human-readable `FACTION_KNOWLEDGE_PACKS.md` semantics, and a worked non-canonical example). Added `knowledge_packs/schema/faction_pack.schema.json` (required: `manifest`+`faction`; optional: `hull_archetypes`/`retrofit_templates`/`progression_tiers`/`capability_gap_guidance`/`officer_guidance`/`notes`; section-level `basis[]`+`confidence` provenance per guidance entry rather than one pack-wide number; `schema_version`/`pack_version`/`target_mod_version` kept as three separate fields per the owner's explicit "that distinction will save pain later"), `knowledge_packs/examples/hmi.example.json` (marked `example_only: true`, `authorship_method: AI_ASSISTED_REVIEW`), and `core/knowledge_packs.py` (`load_knowledge_pack`, `assess_pack_freshness`, `resolve_knowledge_pack`). Verified the example's own content against real game files before trusting it, exactly as this project's numeric claims always are: `roach_king` is confirmed a real HMI hull ("Roach King", Heavy Junker Cruiser); the mod/faction id was initially guessed as lowercase `"hmi"` and corrected to the real uppercase `"HMI"` after checking the live install's own `mod_info.json` and `hmi.faction`; `manifest.source_hashes` in the example are the real SHA-256 hashes of this install's actual `hmi.faction`/`ship_data.csv` files, not placeholders. Writing the freshness-assessment code caught a real bug before any test ran: `Registry` has no `mods` field at all (`ScanResult.mods` is discarded once `Registry.from_scan` builds the entity indexes), so an initial `hasattr(registry, "mods")` check would have silently no-opped forever; fixed by deriving mod presence from real entity `source_mod` evidence across every index instead. Implemented graceful reference degradation per the owner's explicit rule ("pack can recommend unavailable things, but loader must resolve them"): an unresolved `hull_id` drops only that one entry into `unresolved_references`, never the whole pack. 14 new tests (`tests/test_knowledge_packs.py`), including one that loads the real `hmi.example.json` file itself. Full suite: 181 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: the HMI mod is genuinely disabled in this specific install (confirmed via `enabled_mods.json`), so the loader correctly reports `INCOMPATIBLE` (target faction doesn't resolve) and correctly marks both real `hull_archetypes`/`retrofit_templates` entries as unresolved rather than fabricating a result -- this is a real end-to-end validation of the "mod not currently available" path against genuine data, not a synthetic stand-in. Not implemented in this slice: consuming a pack's guidance anywhere (the Gap Recommendation Engine's retrofit/acquisition legs, which this would bias, don't exist yet -- see `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md` section 12), and `APPROVED`/`RESTRICTED`/`UNKNOWN` states aren't used by the equipment-affinity classifier yet (that classifier predates this pack framework and doesn't consult it).

(1) Added `svg query fighters` and `svg query hullmods` -- `classify_fighter`/`classify_hullmod` (both already existed, tested, but had zero CLI surface, unlike weapons/hulls) plus the new equipment-affinity classifier, mirroring the `query weapons`/`query hulls` pattern exactly. Added `Registry.fighters_matching(role)`/`hullmods_matching(hidden)` (mirroring `weapons_matching`/`hulls_matching`) to support them. (2) Wired `--overrides-dir` into `svg export` (`api.py::run_export` gained the same `weapon_role_overrides` parameter `run_generate` already had) -- export previously had no way to apply the weapons Manual Override Layer at all, even though `generate` already did. 2 new registry tests (`tests/test_scanner.py::RegistryMatchingTests`). Full suite: 183 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: `query fighters` returned 423 real fighter wings with correct `role_tags`/`faction_affinity`; `query hullmods --hidden-only` returned 983 real hidden hullmods with correct `property_tags`/`faction_affinity`; `export lasher --profile PD_ESCORT` ran cleanly both with the default (no-op) overrides directory and with a real override file present.

- 2026-08-22: Started root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 9 (Refit Assistant), first slice: `FIX_LEGALITY` mode only, of the 7 modes `HULLMODS_CIVILIAN_AND_REFIT.md` sections 12-19 name (the other 6 -- `REDUCE_FLUX`/`IMPROVE_AI_FIT`/`IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH`/`IMPROVE_LOGISTICS`/`IMPROVE_SURVEY`/`IMPROVE_SALVAGE`/`BALANCED_IMPROVEMENT` -- need a real quality-gain-per-change-cost optimizer weighing competing candidate changes against each other; `FIX_LEGALITY` doesn't, since `validate_variant` already gives an unambiguous mechanical target instead of a heuristic one to search toward). `generation/refit.py::fix_legality` maps each real `LegalityFinding` failure code to a dedicated, minimal, deterministic fix (mount/weapon reference cleanup, built-in-override removal, mount-compatibility repair via the cheapest documented-compatible replacement -- falling back to removal only when none exists, hullmod/fighter reference cleanup, logistics-hullmod-cap and fighter-bay-cap trimming to the excess only, flux vent/capacitor clamping, and a greedy highest-OP-first removal loop for `OP_EXCEEDED`), applied in dependency order and re-validated after each pass. Implemented section 15's lock rule (mount/hullmod/wing ids the refit must never touch, even if that leaves the result non-legal) and section 17's "No Silent Rebuild" rule (`refit_max_changes`, `baseline_0.2`, caps total changes; exceeding it reports `rebuild_recommended` rather than continuing indefinitely or quietly calling the full generator). Added section 14's suggested change-cost weights to `core/heuristics.py` (`refit_cost_weapon_change`/`refit_cost_hullmod_change`/`refit_cost_fighter_change`/`refit_cost_vent_cap_change`, plus `refit_max_changes`). Deliberately left `HULLMOD_INCOMPATIBLE` unhandled: `INCOMPATIBLE_HULLMOD_PAIRS` is empty by design (SVG-010), so this code never fires against real data and a fixer for it would be unverifiable. Wired into `api.py::run_fix_legality` and `svg refit <variant_id> --lock-mount/--lock-hullmod/--lock-wing`. 11 new tests (`tests/test_refit.py`), all passing on the first run. Full suite: 194 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: corrupting the real `lasher_Standard` variant with an incompatible energy weapon on a ballistic mount was repaired with exactly one `WEAPON_REPLACED` change back to LEGAL. A broader stress test (80 random real legal variants, each corrupted with one random weapon substitution) restored 66/80 to LEGAL and correctly declined to guess on the other 8, which land in `NOT_DETERMINABLE` (undocumented real mount types like `LAUNCH_BAY`/`DECORATIVE`/`STATION_MODULE`) -- this surfaced a real reporting bug before release: `RefitResult.unresolved_failures` only read `.failures`, so a `NOT_DETERMINABLE` result (whose blocker lives in `.uncertainties`) reported zero reasons; fixed to include both. A full sweep injecting a nonexistent weapon id into all 618 real legal variants with weapons restored 618/618 (100%) to LEGAL, 0 exceptions.

- 2026-08-22: Continued root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 8 with the `ADAPTIVE` substitution mode's scoring engine (`EQUIPMENT_ACCESS_AND_AUTOFIT.md` sections 9-12), a real, standalone next step now that `FIX_LEGALITY`'s own naive "cheapest compatible" replacement logic showed the shape of what a smarter version needs. `analysis/adaptive_substitution.py::score_substitution_candidate`/`rank_substitution_candidates` score a candidate weapon against a target across the components `HEURISTICS.md` section 11/13 names, using `HEURISTICS.md`'s own suggested starting weights transcribed verbatim into `core/heuristics.py` rather than re-invented (`substitution_weight_*`, plus the `affinity_preference_*` table from section 12). Deliberately excluded 2 of the 8 named components: `AI_friendliness` has no classifier anywhere in this project, so it's left out of the weighted average entirely rather than scored as a fabricated 0; `confidence` is computed as the *output* (fraction of the other 6 components that had real data for a given pair), not fed back in as an input, since a result's own reliability isn't a property of the candidate being scored. The other 6 (`role_match`/`range_match`/`flux_match`/`damage_behavior_match`/`op_efficiency`/`affinity`) all reuse already-tested classifiers (`classify_weapon`, `classify_equipment_affinity`) rather than new inference -- missing data excludes a component from both the average and the id set, never zeros it. 6 new tests. Full suite: 200 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: scoring 200 real same-mount-type-and-size candidates against `heavyblaster` ranked `phasebeam` highest (perfect range/damage/OP/affinity match); stress-tested scoring across 60 real weapons x ~30 candidates each (1,800 calls), 0 exceptions. Not yet wired into `FIX_LEGALITY`'s replacement selection (still "cheapest compatible") or generation -- that's a separate, deliberately deferred decision, since swapping FIX_LEGALITY's already-tested, already-verified selection strategy needs its own opt-in path, not a silent behavior change.

- 2026-08-22: Started root `ROADMAP.md` Phase 11 (Recommendation Explainability), first slice: "Why Not?" for the native gap recommendations Phase 10 already computes (`GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md` section 13, previously marked not-implemented -- "native-only has no losing candidates worth explaining beyond 'ranked lower,' which the ranked list already shows"). That reasoning held only while the ranked list stayed internal; extracting it into a queryable answer is real, additive work, not a re-derivation. Refactored `recommend_native_solutions`'s internal "score every known hull for one role" logic into a shared `_rank_candidates_for_role` helper (previously it computed the full ranking and threw away everything past the top `gap_recommendation_count`) so a new `explain_native_candidate(faction, registry, role, hull_id)` can answer "why wasn't this hull recommended" using the exact same real ranking, not a second inference mechanism. Distinguishes three real outcomes rather than collapsing them into one: `recommended` (was in the top N, with its rank), `ranked-but-not-recommended` (a real positive score, but below the cutoff -- reports the exact score gap to the last hull that *was* recommended), and `scores 0.0` (no real evidence of this capability at all, a materially different situation from "ranked last" that the reason text calls out explicitly). An unresolved hull id (not a real known hull of this faction) is reported as `resolved=False` rather than silently treated as a zero-scoring real hull. Wired into `api.py::run_why_not` and `svg why-not <faction_id> <role> <hull_id>`. 4 new tests (`tests/test_gap_recommendation.py::WhyNotExplanationTests`). Full suite: 204 tests passing. Live-verified against the real install: `why-not pirates BATTLE_CARRIER atlas2` correctly reports "Recommended: ranked 1 of 2"; `why-not pirates BATTLE_CARRIER vanguard_pirates` (a real pirate hull with zero carrier-adjacent mounts) correctly reports "no real evidence" rather than a misleading rank; `why-not pirates CARRIER atlas2` correctly reports the same for the genuinely unaddressed `CARRIER` gap. Stress-tested across every real faction's first 5 known hulls x all 5 capability roles (3,590 calls) plus an unresolved-hull-id check per faction: 0 exceptions.

- 2026-08-22: Wired the Adaptive Substitution scoring engine into the Refit Assistant as an opt-in mode, closing a gap flagged in both Phase 8 ("the substitution engine exists but isn't wired into anything yet") and Phase 9 ("not yet wired into ADAPTIVE substitution modes") simultaneously. `generation/refit.py::fix_legality` gained `substitution_mode` ("cheapest", the default, byte-identical to every existing caller, or "adaptive") and `requesting_faction_id`. Refactored `_FIXERS` from a module-level constant into `_build_fixers(...)`, built fresh per call so the mount-compatibility fixer can have that call's `substitution_mode`/`requesting_faction_id` bound in via `functools.partial` -- every other fixer is completely unaffected. In `adaptive` mode, a mount-incompatible weapon is replaced with the real best role/range/flux/damage/affinity match to the *original* weapon (preserving fitting intent, not just picking the cheapest legal option), via `analysis/adaptive_substitution.py::rank_substitution_candidates`. Wired into `api.py::run_fix_legality` and `svg refit --substitution-mode adaptive --faction-id`. 1 new test proving the two modes genuinely diverge (a cheap-but-poor-match candidate loses to a pricier-but-better-match one only in adaptive mode). Full suite: 205 tests passing, all 11 pre-existing refit tests unchanged (confirming the default path is untouched). Live-verified against the real corrupted `lasher_Standard`: cheapest mode picked `SunriderAlliancePD`, adaptive mode picked a genuinely different weapon (`mm_cryospoolpd`), both reaching LEGAL. Re-ran the same real-data stress test in adaptive mode: identical 66/74 legal rate to cheapest mode (expected -- the 8 unfixable cases are `NOT_DETERMINABLE`, unrelated to which substitution strategy is used), 0 exceptions.

- 2026-08-22: Aggregated the civilian OP-efficiency metric into `QualityAssessment`'s score, closing a gap flagged in both Phase 4 ("the per-effect efficiency ratio is not yet aggregated into QualityAssessment's score") and Phase 13 ("no civilian scoring dimensions"). `scoring/candidate_score.py::_civilian_efficiency_component` averages `AppliedLogisticsEffect.efficiency` across a variant's applied LOGISTICS hullmod effects and normalizes it to 0-100 against a new heuristic, `civilian_efficiency_reference` (`baseline_0.2`, 6.0 -- explicitly grounded in real efficiency values already computed this session for `auxiliary_fuel_tanks`/`expanded_cargo_holds`, not an idealized theoretical max, since no such documented constant exists to verify against). Silently absent (no component, no explanation line) for the vast majority of combat variants that carry no logistics hullmods at all -- not a penalty, just not applicable, so it never adds noise to non-civilian reports. `baseline_0.1`'s legacy 3-component branch is completely untouched (the golden regression test still passes unchanged). Deliberately scoped out: the existing "no weapons -> flat 0.0, no quality recommendation" early-return path (which many real civilian ships with zero weapon mounts would hit) doesn't consider civilian_efficiency at all -- extending that path is a separate, real behavior change to already-tested logic, not attempted here. 4 new tests (`tests/test_scoring.py`), including an exact-math check (gain 30 / OP 5 = 6.0 efficiency = exactly the reference value = a perfect 100.0 score) and a `baseline_0.1`-unaffected check. Full suite: 208 tests passing.
