Noted in the file itself that a future pack refresh will overwrite this back to a blank template, and that root `ROADMAP.md` remains the actively-maintained copy to re-sync from.

- 2026-08-22: Added a genuine regression anchor for `doctrine_match`'s exact `baseline_0.2` arithmetic (`tests/test_doctrine.py`, pins a partial-mismatch scenario's score to 0.562, not just the trivial 1.0-match/None-evidence cases already covered), addressing the "no representative benchmark suite covering doctrine_match's heuristic weights" gap honestly: this is regression *stability* protection (a future formula change gets caught), explicitly not presented as *calibration* against ground truth, since no labeled "this variant doctrine-matches well" dataset exists or could be built without fabricating one. Full suite: 209 tests passing. Also reassessed Phase 11's two remaining named gaps (solution diversity, per-candidate confidence) and concluded neither is honestly achievable right now without either more phases (diversity needs the retrofit/acquisition legs to provide genuinely different solution *categories* -- native-only recommendations don't have a real second axis to diversify across) or fabricating an evidentiary basis that doesn't exist (a per-candidate confidence figure `classify_hull`'s role-compatibility formula has no internal concept of). Recorded as a considered, evidence-based stopping point rather than an oversight -- the same category of decision as civilian classification stopping at evidence tags.

- 2026-08-23: Implemented 4 of the 6 remaining Refit Assistant quality-improvement modes (root ROADMAP.md Phase 9): `generation/refit.py::improve_quality` (`BALANCED_IMPROVEMENT`, `REDUCE_FLUX`, `IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH`, `IMPROVE_LOGISTICS`), `svg refit <variant_id> --mode ... --profile ...`, `api.py::run_improve_quality`. Design: HULLMODS_CIVILIAN_AND_REFIT.md section 14's `quality_gain / change_cost` greedy search, reusing `scoring/candidate_score.py::score_candidate`'s existing components (`final_score`/`flux_sustainability`/`role_match`/`civilian_efficiency`) as the target metric for each mode rather than inventing a new one; every candidate change is independently re-validated LEGAL before being considered, so quality search can never trade legality for score, matching FIX_LEGALITY's own hard boundary. `REDUCE_FLUX` is guarded to reject any candidate that would regress `role_match` or `range_coherence` ("preserving role/range" per section 13's own wording). Added heuristic `refit_min_quality_gain` (baseline_0.2) so the search doesn't thrash on negligible/rounding-noise gains. Found and fixed a real design bug before it shipped: `IMPROVE_LOGISTICS`'s starting `civilian_efficiency` is legitimately `None` (absent, not zero) on any variant with no logistics hullmod installed yet -- exactly the common starting case this mode exists to search from -- so a naive "start metric must be non-None" precondition would have blocked the mode's entire primary use case; fixed by treating that specific absence as a real `0.0` baseline for `IMPROVE_LOGISTICS` only (other modes still correctly treat a `None` metric as "not applicable, nothing to search toward"). The other 3 named modes (`IMPROVE_AI_FIT`, `IMPROVE_SURVEY`, `IMPROVE_SALVAGE`) are documented in `UNIMPLEMENTED_QUALITY_MODES` as genuinely not implementable today (no AI-friendliness classifier anywhere in this project; `classify_civilian_role`'s real scanned hull hints never include SURVEY or SALVAGE), raising a clear `ValueError` rather than silently no-opping. 8 new tests in `tests/test_refit.py`. Live-verified against the real 148-mod install across `lasher_Standard` (REDUCE_FLUX correctly reported "not evaluated" since this hull's real flux data isn't fully documented; BALANCED_IMPROVEMENT and IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH both ran cleanly to a real, unforced 0-change result) and `phantom_Elite` (IMPROVE_LOGISTICS ran cleanly to a real 0-change result), ~15s wall time for a full-install BALANCED_IMPROVEMENT run -- acceptable for a one-off CLI command. Full suite: 216 tests passing.

- 2026-08-23: Implemented Phase 14's "detailed scoring overrides" gap (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md row 7): Advanced-mode JSON requests may now include `scoring_weight_overrides` (`profiles/advanced.py::_scoring_weight_overrides`), letting a user rebalance `score_candidate`'s own final weighted-average component weights for a single generation run. Deliberately scoped to stay inside Agent.md's "every consulted heuristic lives in the named, versioned registry" rule: the override is restricted to a fixed allow-list of exactly the 6 already-registered `baseline_0.2` `weight_*` keys (`weight_range_coherence`/`weight_op_efficiency`/`weight_role_match`/`weight_flux_sustainability`/`weight_faction_doctrine`/`weight_civilian_efficiency`) -- a request naming any other key (threshold, target, ...) is rejected with a clear error, matching how Advanced requests already reject unsupported weapon-restriction fields. `score_candidate` gained an optional `weight_overrides` parameter (default `None`, byte-identical existing behavior) that merges into the resolved heuristic values before scoring; applied overrides are recorded in the assessment's own `explanation` and (via `api.py::run_generate`) in the generation report's `advanced_request`, matching Agent.md's "report the exact heuristic_set and resolved values" rule. `baseline_0.1`'s legacy branch is unaffected by construction (it never reads `weight_*` keys). 6 new tests (`tests/test_advanced.py`, `tests/test_scoring.py`), including an exact-math check confirming a specific override changes a hand-computed final score from 89.3 to 52.6. Live-verified against the real install (`svg generate lasher --mode advanced --advanced-config <file with scoring_weight_overrides>`): the override was correctly loaded, applied, and reported end-to-end. Full suite: 222 tests passing.

- 2026-08-23: Three concurrent workstreams landed together, none conflicting (verified: 247 tests passing after merge, 0 regressions).
  1. **Gap Recommendation Engine (Phase 10), RETROFIT + ACQUISITION legs.** Own work. `analysis/gap_recommendation.py::recommend_gap_solutions` combines the existing native leg with two new ones. RETROFIT (`recommend_retrofit_solutions`) required first correcting a wrong assumption: `classify_hull`'s capability axes are purely structural (mount composition), so no refit can raise a hull's capability_score -- the real, honest v1 target (per FACTION_KNOWLEDGE_PACKS.md section 14, "search native retrofit solutions") is instead: among the same structurally-capable native hulls the native leg already finds, does that hull's real, currently-fitted variant realize that potential, or would `generation/refit.py::improve_quality`'s `IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH` mode meaningfully close a loadout-quality gap on top of it? Reuses the native leg's own ranked pool and the Refit Assistant's own real quality delta -- no new inference mechanism. Requires an explicit, documented mapping from `classify_hull`'s 5 capability axes to `profiles/catalog.py`'s profile ids (`_ROLE_TO_PROFILE`) since the two vocabularies were never unified; BATTLE_CARRIER/CARRIER both map to CARRIER_SUPPORT (no dedicated profile exists for the hybrid axis). ACQUISITION (`recommend_acquisition_solutions`) required extending `analysis/equipment_affinity.py::classify_equipment_affinity` to `entity_kind="hulls"` (a 2-line change -- the existing mechanism already generalizes cleanly since a hull is real faction-ownership evidence in the same shape as a weapon/fighter/hullmod), then ranking non-native hulls by `capability_score * affinity_preference_<tier>` using the already-registered `baseline_0.2` preference table, directly implementing FACTION_KNOWLEDGE_PACKS.md section 9's "foreign acquisitions need a clear capability advantage" without inventing a new doctrine-strictness mechanism. `GapRecommendationResult` gained 3 new fields, all backward-compatible defaults, so `recommend_native_solutions` and every existing test calling it directly are untouched. `unaddressed_gaps` (no native solution) is kept as-is; a new `fully_unaddressed_gaps` (no solution across all 3 legs) implements section 15's own "full maturity" definition properly. Live-verified against real Pirates data: 3 gaps, 1 without a native solution (CARRIER), 0 fully unaddressed -- real acquisition candidates surfaced from real installed mods (`armaa_*`, `IndEvo_artilleryStation_*`, `SunriderACV*`) with correct real affinity classifications and preference weights. Also discovered live (not fabricated away): `IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH`'s greedy single-step search structurally can't fix `role_match`'s all-or-nothing "every weapon must satisfy the range threshold" formula once more than one mounted weapon already violates it, so real multi-weapon combat variants (e.g. Pirates' own `atlas2_Standard`, 14 mounted weapons) rarely retrofit today -- confirmed by checking real analyze-variant output, not assumed. Documented honestly as a real, inherited limitation rather than silently shipped as "it just doesn't apply here." 10 new tests across `tests/test_equipment_affinity.py` and `tests/test_gap_recommendation.py`.
  2. **Hullmod Effect Engine (Phase 4), DEFENSE category.** Delegated to a background agent, verified after landing. Added `adapters/vanilla/__init__.py::DEFENSE_HULLMOD_EFFECTS` (4 real hullmods: `heavyarmor`, `armoredweapons`, `reinforcedhull`, `blast_doors`, each OP cost cross-checked exactly against the live install), `analysis/combat_stats.py::compute_derived_defense_stats` (new module, mirrors `compute_derived_civilian_stats`'s discipline exactly: absence of verified data is never absence of effect; same-stat stacking ambiguity across 2+ hullmods is left unresolved with an explanatory note rather than guessed at, since vanilla's real stacking rule for e.g. two independent hull-HP-percent hullmods is undocumented anywhere verifiable), and a one-level skin-hull fallback for base stats (verified against real `afflictor_d_pirates` -> `afflictor` skin data). Surfaced via `svg analyze-variant`'s new `defense_stats` field. Stress-tested across all 5,526 real variants in the live install, 0 exceptions. Deliberately not wired into `scoring/candidate_score.py` or `generation/refit.py` -- reserved as a separate integration step. 15 new tests.
  3. **Refit Assistant (Phase 9), EXACT/STARSECTOR_STYLE substitution modes.** Delegated to a background agent, verified after landing. Read `EQUIPMENT_ACCESS_AND_AUTOFIT.md` section 9 for the real definitions before implementing (project rule: never infer undocumented behavior) -- `EXACT` ("reproduce specified IDs exactly, no substitution, missing items are reported") never substitutes at all, even when a compatible replacement exists (`_exact_compatible_weapon` always returns `None`); `STARSECTOR_STYLE` ("preserve slot/category/group intent") ranks by `classify_weapon` role-tag overlap then range-band match then closest OP cost, a deliberately lighter mechanism than `ADAPTIVE`'s full weighted score (no new heuristic was warranted -- a plain lexicographic tie-break over already-tested fields needed no calibration). `--substitution-mode` now accepts `cheapest`/`exact`/`starsector_style`/`adaptive`. Live-verified against a real `wasp_Interceptor` mount-type-mismatch case: all 4 modes produced genuinely distinct, individually correct selections. 2 new tests.

  Full suite after all three: 247 tests passing, 0 failures.

- 2026-08-23: Extended Recommendation Explainability (Phase 11) to cover all 3 implemented Gap Recommendation Engine legs, not just native. `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` gained `explain_retrofit_candidate` (distinguishes: not considered -- outside the native leg's own top-ranked shortlist retrofit search examines / considered but no real indexed variant / considered with real variant(s) but none improve `role_match` by `refit_min_quality_gain` / recommended, with the real before/after role_match and which specific variant) and `explain_acquisition_candidate` (distinguishes: unresolved hull id / already-native, so acquisition doesn't apply -- see native/retrofit instead / zero real capability evidence / ranked-but-below-cutoff with the real score gap / recommended, with real affinity and rank), plus a combining `explain_candidate`/`CombinedWhyNotExplanation` that answers all 3 in one call, matching how a real user actually wants "why wasn't this recommended?" answered (not three separate lookups that could silently disagree). Every number is read directly off the exact same real ranked lists `recommend_retrofit_solutions`/`recommend_acquisition_solutions` themselves compute -- same "no second inference mechanism" discipline `explain_native_candidate` already established. `api.py::run_why_not`'s return type changed from `WhyNotExplanation` to `CombinedWhyNotExplanation`; `svg why-not`'s CLI report and console output updated to show all 3 legs' reasons. `explain_native_candidate` itself, and every existing test calling it directly, are completely unchanged. 9 new tests in `tests/test_gap_recommendation.py`. Live-verified against real Pirates data end-to-end consistent with `svg recommend`'s own output for the same faction: `armaa_bassline` on CARRIER (not native, not retrofit-considered, acquisition rank 1 of 826) and `atlas2` on LINE_ARTILLERY (native rank 1, retrofit considered but no real gain found -- a live instance of the same multi-weapon `role_match` limitation documented alongside the retrofit leg itself, already-native so acquisition doesn't apply). Full suite: 255 tests passing.

- 2026-08-23: Completed the backend-hardening workflow steps before GUI work. The CI-equivalent command is now documented as `uv run --no-project --with-editable . python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; stale status claims about survivability scoring were reconciled. Verified DEFENSE hullmod effects now inform TANK generation and BALANCED_IMPROVEMENT refits only when an adapter supplies a computable effect; Advanced mode accepts the registered survivability weight. Knowledge packs gained validated `approved_equipment` guidance, exposed through `svg generate --knowledge-pack` and `svg recommend --knowledge-pack`; it may classify an item as APPROVED and reduces stale guidance confidence, but cannot change hard legality. `IMPROVE_ROLE_MATCH` now crosses the all-or-nothing multi-weapon range plateau via bounded legal progress moves. Native, retrofit, and acquisition recommendation records expose separate confidence values. Regression coverage was added across these paths; full suite: 268 tests passing.

- 2026-08-23: Continued Phase 7/10/11 integration: `capability_gap_guidance` is now emitted only for the pack's target faction and matching role, with its declared confidence attenuated by pack freshness (`CURRENT`/`PARTIALLY_STALE`/`STALE`) and never used as a legality or structural-capability input. `svg why-not --knowledge-pack` now shares the same pack-aware acquisition-affinity context as `svg recommend --knowledge-pack`, preventing a recommendation and its explanation from using different rankings. Focused knowledge-pack/recommendation tests: 46 passing; prior full suite: 269 passing.

- 2026-08-23: GUI general pass. Fixed two real, reproducible crashes in the Retrofits workspace ("Improve Role Match"/"Compare Before / After" passed a non-existent `mode="guided"`/`profile="LINE"` into `api.run_improve_quality`/`api.run_analyze_variant`, raising `ValueError: Unknown profile: LINE` on every click -- verified against real data before and after). Fixed `_generate`/`_export_current` silently hardcoding the literal `"baseline_0.2"` instead of the real configured `AppConfig.heuristic_set` (default `baseline_0.7`), which meant every GUI generation/export ran under a stale scoring formula with no visible indication why. Added a `QPushButton:disabled` stylesheet rule (previously fell back to Qt's default grey against the custom dark theme) and removed dead connect/disconnect churn on the hull-search field. Diagnosed and fixed a real, pre-existing test-isolation bug (unrelated to the above): `tests/test_gui_canvas.py` had no QSettings isolation, so a test that left `mode_selector` on `"advanced"` and called `window.close()` persisted that into the real, machine-native `("VoidSmith", "Desktop")` settings store -- the same one an actual installed copy uses -- and leaked into whichever test constructed the next `MainWindow()`, deterministically by alphabetical test order. Fixed with per-class `QSettings.setPath(IniFormat, ...)` redirection to a temp file plus a per-test `.clear()`, isolating GUI tests from both the real OS settings store and each other.

  Added drag-and-drop mod loading (`core/mod_import.py`, new): dropping a mod folder or `.zip` archive anywhere onto the main window adds it to the next scan's source pool alongside the normal core + enabled-mods set (`AppConfig.extra_mod_paths` -> `core/scanner.py::Scanner.extra_mod_paths`), without needing the mod installed under `<starsector_path>/mods/` or listed in `enabled_mods.json` at all -- useful for testing a mod-in-development or one not yet installed. Explicitly additive, not exclusive: an earlier "only load this mod" framing was walked back by the project owner mid-implementation in favor of "add it to the data pool" once the Scanner's real discovery mechanism (core + enabled_mods.json-driven mod list) was understood -- isolating to one mod would have meant reinventing that discovery path with different semantics for no real benefit, whereas adding an extra source alongside it reuses the exact same per-source scan/merge/cache machinery every other mod already goes through. A dropped `.zip` is extracted only into a program-controlled cache directory under the configured output dir (never beside Starsector or existing mod sources), with zip-slip path-traversal rejected before any file is written, and re-dropping an updated archive with the same name refreshes the extraction rather than leaving stale files. Settings tab gained an "Additional Mods" list with add/remove. 21 new tests (`tests/test_mod_import.py`: 10; `tests/test_scanner.py`: 1; `tests/test_gui_canvas.py`: 2) plus the QSettings-isolation fix. Live-verified against the real 148-mod install: extracted and scanned a real installed mod folder as an "extra" source end-to-end with 0 exceptions. Full suite: 378 tests passing, 1 skipped.

- 2026-08-23: Batch review pass (orphaned code, parallelism, build pipeline), three independent audits, plus the final packaged compile. Full suite after all three landed: 376 tests passing, 1 skipped (2 fewer than the 378 above -- one test file was deleted along with the dead code it only existed to exercise, nothing that used to pass now fails).

  **Orphaned code**, via AST-based whole-repo reference counting (excluding `gui/`, verified separately): removed `analysis/control_suitability.py` (confirmed still fully unwired, zero references anywhere -- corroborates the earlier GUI-review finding), `analysis/capability_objectives.py` (superseded by the actually-wired `analysis/scenario_objectives.py`), `tests/test_evidence_and_objectives.py` (only exercised the two removed pieces), and two dead module-level constants (`core/evidence.py::EVIDENCE_STATES`, `analysis/change_impact.py::CHANGE_STATUSES`). Investigated and deliberately kept several low-reference-count modules that turned out to be legitimate: `core/result_cache.py` and `core/scan_profiles.py` are documented, intentionally-partial infrastructure (`ROADMAP.md` Phase 2's own stated "candidate/recommendation result reuse remains deferred" and `docs/TRIOS_ARCHITECTURE_REUSE_REVIEW.md`'s open follow-up), not dead code; `analysis/calibration_runner.py`/`analysis/complex_hull_audit.py`/`core/mod_import.py` all have real callers in `tools/`/`gui/` despite no `src`-internal caller.

  **Parallelism**, following the same measure-first discipline `core/scanner.py`'s own prior (already-settled, not re-litigated) finding established -- serial scanning beat threaded scanning on real data. Measured `calibration_runner.py`'s batch build-generation on a real 200-hull sample: thread-pool gave no benefit (GIL-bound, matching the scanner's finding); a process-pool gave a real ~1.7-1.9x speedup *at N=200*, but at the real current calibration corpus size (12 labels) the same approach measured *worse* than serial (process-spawn/import overhead dominates a batch that small) -- correctly not applied. `tools/audit_complex_hulls.py` measured at 13ms on the full real install, nothing to parallelize. Found the actual win via profiling instead: `generation/candidate.py::_build_candidate` was rescanning all ~3,200 registry weapons with a fresh `.upper()` call for every mount on every hull (`O(mounts x total_weapons)`), 44% of batch-generation time. Fixed by grouping weapons by normalized mount type once per call, before the mount loop -- a pure restructuring of the same eligibility predicate, not a behavior change. Measured: 33.24ms/hull -> 19.51ms/hull, a 1.7x speedup, verified against 2,100 real hull/profile combinations (all 7 profiles x 300 real hulls) with 0 mismatches in selected weapons, legality, or omissions versus the original implementation.

  **Build pipeline**: found ~19MB of `Qt6Quick`/`Qt6Qml*`/`Qt6VirtualKeyboard` DLLs and 96 unused Qt translation files (6.44MB) in a real built release -- pulled in only because PySide6's `QtGui` PyInstaller hook always bundles the virtual-keyboard input-method plugin (confirmed via `PyInstaller.utils.hooks.qt.add_qt6_dependencies`, which returns none of these for `QtWidgets`/`QtGui` directly) and because Qt's own translations are bundled regardless of whether `QTranslator` is ever used (it isn't, anywhere in `src/`). Added a guarded prune to `tools/build_portable_release.ps1` that deletes both after the release copy, re-checking the real source tree on every build for `QtQuick|QtQml|QVirtualKeyboard|setInputMethodHints`/`QTranslator|installTranslator` first and skipping the prune (with a warning) if a future change makes it unsafe. Rejected two other candidates with real justification: image-format plugins (webp/tiff/gif etc., ~1.8MB) are reachable, since `gui/resources.py` loads arbitrary mod-supplied sprite files by extension; `QtNetwork`/OpenSSL (~9.5MB) are unconditionally imported by PySide6's own `__init__.py` on Windows just to preload OpenSSL DLLs -- removing them breaks `import PySide6` itself. Measured result on a real built release: onedir 116.91MB -> 97.5MB (-16.6%), zip 48.3MB -> 40.6MB (-15.9%), 213 files -> 110, both in-place and post-extraction smoke tests still passing. UPX investigated and correctly left disabled (the binary isn't available/lockable in this offline pipeline; PyInstaller silently no-ops `upx=True` when it's absent, confirmed via its own source). Incremental-build caching already gives a real ~50x speedup on a warm cache (696ms vs ~32-38s), confirmed via existing build logs, no further work needed there.

  Separately found and fixed a real correctness bug while investigating: `dist/build_voidsmith.bat` -- the actual entry point a user runs -- called `tools/build_gui_exe.ps1`, which used `uv run --with pyinstaller` (network-resolved, unpinned PyInstaller) rather than the locked `6.22.2` `uv.lock` pins the real release pipeline (`tools/build_portable_release.ps1`) uses, and built `--onefile` without the Qt-pruning step above at all. Fixed `build_gui_exe.ps1` to use the identical `uv sync --locked --offline --extra gui --group release` / `uv run --locked --offline --no-sync ... pyinstaller` pattern as the release script (with the same explicit, reviewed `-BootstrapDependencies` escape hatch), preserving its onefile shape and role as the fast local build (`ROADMAP.md` Phase 0 documents both a one-file build and the versioned zip release as separate, intentional deliverables -- not redirected to the other pipeline).

  **Final compile** (after all of the above landed): cleared `dist/` and rebuilt both real deliverables. `tools/build_portable_release.ps1 -FreshAnalysis` -> `VoidSmith-0.1.0-win-x64.zip` (40,623,377 bytes, SHA-256 `41236c5d...`), matching the build-agent's measured optimized size. Verified directly against the extracted files (not just trusted the prune logic): the built release contains exactly 7 real Qt6 DLLs (`Core`/`Gui`/`Network`/`OpenGL`/`Pdf`/`Svg`/`Widgets`) and zero matches for Bluetooth/serial-port/positioning/multimedia/NFC/sensor/WebEngine/Quick/QML/VirtualKeyboard DLLs, and zero `.qm` translation files -- confirms the pruning actually took effect in the real shipped artifact, not just in the pipeline's intent. `dist/build_voidsmith.bat` (now fixed) -> `VoidSmith.exe` (48,161,090 bytes) -- down from 399,627,433 bytes before today's fix, an 8x reduction almost certainly from the prior unpinned/unlocked `uv run --with pyinstaller` resolving a substantially different, bloated dependency set rather than this run's clean, locked one; smoke-tested via the exe's own built-in `--smoke-test` flag (`tools/verify_gui_exe.ps1`), exit 0. Full suite re-confirmed green after the whole pass: 376 passing, 1 skipped.

- 2026-08-23: Fixed a real, user-reported scan-progress bug: `core/scanner.py::scan()` reported zero progress for the entire real duration of a scan (fingerprint-checking every source's files, then waiting on every parse future) and only emitted all per-source progress events in one instantaneous burst afterward, in a separate merge loop that ran once every fragment was already fully parsed. To a user watching the GUI's progress dialog, this was indistinguishable from a hang -- confirmed by direct measurement against the real 148-mod install: all 119 "PARSING" progress events landed at the exact same timestamp (19.97s into a ~20s scan), with the label frozen at its initial state the entire time before that. Fixed by moving progress emission into the two phases that actually do the work (the fingerprint/cache-check loop, and the future-result-collection loop), using a new `Scanner._fragment_entity_count` helper to report a real, incrementally growing entity count from each not-yet-merged fragment (merging itself is unchanged, still happens afterward in guaranteed stable order for deterministic output -- only *when progress is reported* changed, not scan correctness or output). Re-measured against the real install: progress now ticks roughly every 2-4 seconds across nearly the whole scan (a real ~6.6s gap remains at the very start, while "core" -- one large, indivisible source -- parses; a smaller, much less suspicious pause than the prior ~20-24s total freeze). `_emit_progress` gained an optional `entities_found` override (defaulting to its prior `result`-based computation, used unchanged by the post-merge `RESOLVING_REFERENCES`/`COMPLETE` calls) so this could be reported before merge without behavior-changing anything downstream. `gui/main_window.py::_scan_progressed` was also fixed to switch the scan `QProgressDialog` from permanently indeterminate (`min=0, max=0`, a spinner with no real bar) to determinate once a real total is known, giving a real filling bar and counter, not just an updating text label -- the second half of what was actually needed to *see* the fix, per the project owner's own suggestion mid-diagnosis.

  Also began iterating the project's own version with every real compile, per explicit direction. Bumped `pyproject.toml` to `0.1.1` and discovered two real, would-otherwise-recur build-pipeline gaps while doing the first bumped build: (1) `uv sync --locked --offline` refuses to proceed once `pyproject.toml`'s version no longer matches the version already recorded in `uv.lock` for the local editable `voidsmith` package -- fixed by updating that one line in `uv.lock` to match (the fix for *this* bump; future bumps need the same one-line update, or a `uv lock --offline` equivalent once the environment can support it); (2) even with a matching lock, `uv sync` still tried to rebuild the local editable package's metadata in a fresh, isolated build environment (needing `setuptools`, which wasn't available in the offline package index cache even though it's already installed in `.venv`) -- fixed by adding `--no-build-isolation` to both `uv sync` invocations in `tools/build_portable_release.ps1` and `tools/build_gui_exe.ps1`, reusing the already-installed, already-correct `setuptools` in the project's own venv instead of trying to fetch an isolated copy. Both fixes are permanent (in the scripts/lockfile), not one-off workarounds -- the next version bump should build cleanly without hitting either issue again, provided the same packages stay installed.

  Rebuilt both release artifacts as `0.1.1` after all of the above: `VoidSmith-0.1.1-win-x64.zip` (40,622,984 bytes, SHA-256 `d4178986...`) and `VoidSmith.exe` (48,162,207 bytes), both smoke-tested clean. Full suite: 376 passing, 1 skipped.

- 2026-08-23: Wired real result-cache reuse into Gap Recommendation Engine calls (`analysis/gap_recommendation.py::recommend_gap_solutions`, via `api.py::run_gap_recommendations` and `svg recommend`) -- the "candidate/recommendation result reuse remains deferred" gap `ROADMAP.md` Phase 2 has named all session. First checked the premise rather than trusting it: `core/result_cache.py` (`AnalysisResultCache`/`AnalysisContextFingerprint`/`CacheReadiness`) had **zero** production consumers anywhere in `src/` -- the hull/faction/weapon/hullmod reuse the roadmap already credited to Phase 2 turned out to be a separate, parallel, hand-rolled fingerprinting mechanism in `output/analysis_reports.py` that never imports `core/result_cache.py` at all. This is genuinely the first real consumer of the SQLite cache.

  Chose `recommend_gap_solutions` over candidate generation after tracing both call graphs: candidate generation's correctness surface (whole weapon/hullmod/fighter registry plus 11 constraint parameters including `EntityOverride` objects) was judged a messier first cut than gap recommendations, whose real dependency surface -- confirmed by tracing every adapter it touches -- is the whole registry but *only* fixed, hand-authored Python tables (`adapters.vanilla`), never scanned Java source, making a complete and honest fingerprint achievable now. `gap_recommendation_fingerprint` hashes: every entity's real `source_hash` across all 6 registry categories (fails closed to `CACHE_UNSAFE_INCOMPLETE_CONTEXT` if any is missing -- exercised, not theoretical, since no test fixture in this repo sets `source_hash` explicitly); the heuristic_set; a fixed adapter-version marker for the static tables; a hash of the full resolved knowledge-pack content (not just its freshness label, since two `CURRENT` packs can differ in content); and a constraints hash. Caught and fixed a real bug before shipping: `AnalysisResultCache`'s table keys on `cache_key` alone, so two different factions over an otherwise-identical registry would have silently collided and overwritten each other's cached result unless the target faction's own identity (`faction.id` + `faction.source_hash`) is folded into the hashed context, not just passed as the cache's separate `target_id` column -- a dedicated regression test now proves this doesn't happen.

  Measured on real data (148-mod install, 2996 hulls/3191 weapons/1303 hullmods/5566 variants/120 factions), repeated in-process calls against one already-built `Registry`: `xlu` (34 known hulls, real acquisition-leg work) 0.428s fresh -> 0.006s cached, ~71x; `zea_dawn` 0.368s -> 0.006s, ~61x; `ironsentinel` (native-leg-only, already cheap) 0.031s -> 0.004s, ~7x. Cross-process persistence verified directly too: two separate `python` invocations sharing one SQLite file went 0.075s cold -> 0.005s warm. Honest caveat stated plainly rather than oversold: `svg recommend`'s own end-to-end wall time barely moves (~6.2s either way) because each CLI invocation still does a full fresh scan first (out of this change's scope) -- the real win is for repeated calls against one already-scanned registry, e.g. a GUI session or the calibration runner, which is exactly the scenario this was asked to speed up.

  8 new tests (`tests/test_gap_recommendation_result_cache.py`): cache-hit-returns-identical-result (with a call-count spy proving the real function is genuinely not re-invoked, not just coincidentally equal), cache-miss-on-changed-hull-input, cache-miss-on-changed-heuristic-set, incomplete-context-never-served-or-persisted, the cross-faction-collision regression, and a no-op-when-no-cache-given equivalence check. `run_gap_recommendations` gained an optional `result_cache: AnalysisResultCache | None = None` parameter (default `None` reproduces prior behavior exactly for every existing caller); `svg recommend` now constructs a real `output_dir/cache/analysis_results.sqlite`-backed cache. `recommend_gap_solutions` itself and `generation/candidate.py` are both untouched. Candidate-generation caching was deliberately not attempted in this pass -- judged feasible but a less tractable first cut, not ruled out. Full suite: 384 passing (376 + 8 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-23: Added the fourth hullmod-effect category, FLUX (`adapters/vanilla/__init__.py::FLUX_HULLMOD_EFFECTS`, `analysis/flux_stats.py`, following the same LOGISTICS/DEFENSE/MOBILITY pattern) -- targets a hull's `flux_dissipation`/`shield_upkeep` typed fields, the same two `scoring/candidate_score.py::_flux_component` already reads for the existing `flux_sustainability` score (not itself wired into scoring in this pass; noted below as the natural next step). Three verified hullmods modeled, each cross-checked live against the real install's `hull_mods.csv` and the wiki (2026-08-23 fetch): `fluxdistributor` (+30/60/90/150 flux_dissipation by hull size), `safetyoverrides` (x2 flux_dissipation multiplicative factor, excluding CAPITAL_SHIP per its own desc-text restriction), `stabilizedshieldemitter` (-50% shield_upkeep). Ten more real candidates were researched and explicitly excluded with cited reasons rather than silently dropped: `fluxcoil` (targets `flux_capacity`, a third Hull field out of this category's declared scope), `hardenedshieldemitter`/`fluxbreakers` (real effects, but on combat-damage math or a conditional venting-only state this project's static analysis can't represent), `phase_anchor` (conditional on being phased), plus five hidden/narrative-only hullmods (`design_compromises`, `andrada_mods`, `faulty_grid`, `heg_militarized`, `shared_flux_sink`, `fluxshunt`) never player-installable on a generated variant.

  This is the first category where verified entries span three distinct real operation shapes (flat-add, multiplicative factor, percent reduction) rather than one -- `HullmodFluxEffect` and `compute_derived_flux_stats` support all three explicitly rather than force-fitting DEFENSE/MOBILITY's flat-by-size-XOR-additive-percent vocabulary. Same never-fabricate-a-stack discipline as the other three categories: `fluxdistributor` (flat-add) and `safetyoverrides` (multiply) both target `flux_dissipation` and can legitimately co-occur on one variant, so `effective_flux_dissipation` is deliberately `None` whenever more than one verified hullmod applies to the same stat, with each individual contribution preserved in `applied_effects` and the ambiguity explained in `stacking_notes` -- vanilla's real combine order (add-then-multiply vs. multiply-then-add) isn't documented anywhere this project can verify, so it isn't guessed at.

  One real gap was found and fixed while independently re-verifying the data (this work resumed after an earlier attempt was cut off by an account-level rate limit): `HullmodFluxEffect` initially had no way to encode `safetyoverrides`' documented "cannot be installed on civilian or capital ships" restriction, since `validation/legality.py` has no hullmod/hull-size restriction check of its own for it (the restriction lives only in unparseable desc text). Without a fix, a capital-ship variant carrying `safetyoverrides` would have gotten a fabricated x2 bonus it can't actually have in-game. Added `excluded_hull_sizes: frozenset[str]` to the dataclass and gated on it in the consumer; verified live against the real Onslaught hull that the fix correctly leaves `flux_dissipation` at its unmodified base (600) instead of fabricating 1200.

  Wired into `svg analyze-variant`'s output via a new `VariantAnalysis.flux_stats` field (`analysis/variant.py`), auto-serialized through the existing report path with no CLI/api.py changes needed. Live-verified base values for five real hulls (Lasher 140/0.4, Hammerhead 250/0.4, Onslaught 600/0.4, Wolf 150/0.4, Eagle 700/0.4) directly against `ship_data.csv`, all exact matches; applied-effect math manually cross-checked (Lasher `fluxdistributor` 140->170, `safetyoverrides` 140->280, `stabilizedshieldemitter` 0.4->0.2; Onslaught correctly excludes `safetyoverrides` via the capital gate); the flat+multiplicative stacking case exercised on a real Lasher carrying both hullmods, confirming `effective_flux_dissipation` stays `None` while both individual contributions (170 and 280, each against the same base 140) are preserved. Stress pass across all 5,526 real registry variants: 0 exceptions. 11 new tests (`tests/test_flux_stats.py`: 10; `tests/test_variant_analysis.py`: 1). Full suite: 395 passing (384 + 11 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

  Explicitly not attempted, left for a distinct future pass: wiring `DerivedFluxStats` into `scoring/candidate_score.py::_flux_component`'s existing `flux_sustainability` score (which still only reads raw, un-hullmod-adjusted `hull.flux_dissipation`/`shield_upkeep`) and possibly into `generation/vent_cap.py`'s flux-budget math, which touches the same two fields.

- 2026-08-23: Wired real result-cache reuse into candidate generation (`generation/candidate.py::generate_candidate_alternatives` and `generate_build_archetype_candidates`, via `api.py::run_generate` and `svg generate`), the sibling `ROADMAP.md` Phase 2 named as "judged feasible but a less tractable first fingerprint" when gap-recommendation caching shipped earlier today. Followed that exact pattern: colocated `*_fingerprint`/`*_result_to_payload`/`*_result_from_payload` functions, an optional `result_cache: AnalysisResultCache | None = None` on `run_generate` (default reproduces prior behavior exactly for every existing caller), `svg generate` now constructs the same `output_dir/cache/analysis_results.sqlite`-backed cache `svg recommend` does. `_build_candidate`'s internals are untouched -- only the two outer generation functions are wrapped.

  Fingerprint honesty, traced by actually reading every consuming function rather than assuming: `entity_hashes` covers the target hull's own hash plus the *entire* weapon/hullmod/fighter registry (verified none of `_build_candidate`'s eligibility scan, `select_hullmods`, or `select_fighter_wings` narrow to a hull-specific subset); `registry.variants`/`registry.factions`/other hulls are excluded (verified `validate_variant` only ever looks up the one target hull). All allow/deny/preferred/locked weapon/hullmod/wing constraints plus `hull_id`, `profile_id`, `flux_mode`, `search_depth`, `max_candidates` fold into a `constraints_hash`; `hull_id` is deliberately included there (not just relied on as the cache's `target_id`) for the identical collision-safety reason the gap-recommendation fix folded in `faction.id` -- a dedicated test uses two hulls sharing a source hash to prove this. `weapon_role_overrides` (`EntityOverride`) gets a real complete hash, since (verified) it's a plain frozen dataclass with no ambiguous fields, unlike the "no obvious stable serialization" case the task was warned to fail closed on. One honest, non-fabricated finding: `generate_candidate_alternatives` doesn't actually accept a `heuristic_set` parameter at all -- its only heuristic-driven step (`vent_cap.py::allocate_vents_and_capacitors`, called from inside `_build_candidate`) always uses that function's own fixed default, never the caller's setting -- so its fingerprint records that real fixed constant (`CANDIDATE_GENERATION_FIXED_VENT_CAP_HEURISTIC_SET`) rather than inventing a parameter that doesn't exist; `generate_build_archetype_candidates`, which genuinely does consume `heuristic_set` for build inference and near-duplicate collapse, uses the real caller-supplied value instead. Every real parameter of both functions turned out to have a complete, honest serialization -- nothing needed `CACHE_UNSAFE_INCOMPLETE_CONTEXT` structurally; that state is reserved for (and exercised by tests against) a genuinely missing entity `source_hash` or an unresolved hull.

  Measured on real data (148-mod install, 2996 hulls/3191 weapons/1303 hullmods/423 fighters, one already-built `Registry`, every cached result asserted identical to the fresh computation): `candidate_alternatives` 15.0-21.6ms fresh -> 2.1-2.6ms cached (6.7-8.4x); `build_archetype_candidates` 22.6-107.0ms fresh -> 2.4-3.0ms cached (9.5-36.2x, the largest gain on Onslaught's more expensive multi-archetype search). Cross-process persistence verified directly: two separate `python` invocations sharing one SQLite cache file went 24.7ms cold -> 2.1ms warm. `svg generate` smoke-tested twice end-to-end against the real install, confirming the cache file is created and reused. Same honest caveat as the gap-recommendation work: a single fresh CLI invocation's wall time is dominated by the full scan (out of scope here); the real win is repeated calls against one already-built registry.

  15 new tests (`tests/test_candidate_generation_result_cache.py`): fingerprint safety/unsafety/hull-collision-safety for both operations, cache-hit-identical-without-recompute (call-count spy), cache-miss-on-changed-weapon, cache-miss-on-changed-search-depth, incomplete-context-never-served-or-persisted, no-cache-arg-behaves-as-before. Scoring (`score_candidate`'s output) is deliberately not cached -- out of this pass's scope, and depends on `advanced.scoring_weight_overrides`/faction context this fingerprint doesn't cover. Full suite: 410 passing (395 + 15 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions. Independently re-verified (full suite re-run, fingerprint/wiring code read directly): confirmed as reported.

- 2026-08-24: Wired yesterday's verified FLUX hullmod modeling (`analysis/flux_stats.py::compute_derived_flux_stats`) into `scoring/candidate_score.py::_flux_component`'s `flux_sustainability` score -- the explicitly-flagged next step from both prior FLUX entries. A candidate installing `fluxdistributor`/`safetyoverrides` now scores its real, hullmod-adjusted flux dissipation instead of the hull's raw unmodified stat.

  Released as a new heuristic registry entry, `baseline_0.8`, not a change to the existing default -- required by this project's own reproducibility guarantee (`ROADMAP.md` Phase 13: "`baseline_0.1` reproduced byte-for-byte"). `BASELINE_0_8.values = {**BASELINE_0_7.values, "flux_hullmod_adjustment_enabled": 1.0}`, the identical dict-spread pattern every prior baseline bump has used, so every one of `baseline_0.7`'s existing values is untouched; `_flux_component` only calls `compute_derived_flux_stats` when `"flux_hullmod_adjustment_enabled" in heuristics`, following this project's established gate-by-key-presence convention (same shape as `weight_pd_coverage`/`weight_range_coherence`). `core/config.py::AppConfig`'s default `heuristic_set` stays `"baseline_0.7"` -- `baseline_0.8` is available but opt-in, matching how the default was NOT bumped forward on the `0.6`->`0.7` transition either. A regression test proves `baseline_0.7`'s score is byte-for-byte unchanged before/after.

  Stacking case handled explicitly, not fabricated: when 2+ verified hullmods target the same stat, `compute_derived_flux_stats` already returns `effective_flux_dissipation=None` (see yesterday's FLUX entry); `_flux_component` treats that as "fall back to the raw base value, with an explanation noting stacking made the effect unrepresentable" -- never a value between the two verified numbers. Live-verified against a real Hammerhead (base `flux_dissipation=250.0`) carrying 4 real `pdlaser`s (sustained load 160.4): no hullmod -> ratio `1.56` under both baselines; `+fluxdistributor` alone -> `baseline_0.8` correctly computes `310.0/160.4=1.93` (matching `compute_derived_flux_stats`'s independently-reported `250->310` exactly) while `baseline_0.7` stays at `1.56`; `+fluxdistributor+safetyoverrides` together -> `baseline_0.8` correctly falls back to the raw-base `1.56` with the stacking note attached, `baseline_0.7` unaffected throughout.

  6 new tests (`tests/test_config_and_heuristics.py`: 2 -- baseline_0.8 byte-identity to 0.7, shipped default unchanged; `tests/test_scoring.py`: 4 -- 0.7 unaffected, 0.8 applies fluxdistributor, 0.8 matches base with no hullmod, 0.8 stacking fallback). `HEURISTICS.md` documents the new entry per its existing convention. Full suite: 416 passing (410 + 6 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions. Independently re-verified (full suite re-run, gating logic and config default read directly). `generation/vent_cap.py`'s own flux-budget math (which also touches these two Hull fields) remains a separate, not-yet-attempted follow-on.

- 2026-08-24: Rebuilt both release artifacts as `0.1.2` (candidate-generation cache reuse + FLUX hullmod modeling/scoring, all three entries above), per the standing per-compile version-iteration practice. Bumped `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock`'s embedded `voidsmith` package version together (the same one-line `uv.lock` fix discovered during the `0.1.1` build). Both `tools/build_portable_release.ps1 -FreshAnalysis` and `dist/build_voidsmith.bat` completed cleanly with no lock/build-isolation errors this time -- the fixes from the `0.1.1` build held. `VoidSmith-0.1.2-win-x64.zip` (41,582,458 bytes, SHA-256 `95ee0982...`) and `VoidSmith.exe` (49,243,643 bytes, SHA-256 `b2b59ff0...`), both smoke-tested clean (`tools/verify_gui_exe.ps1` for the onefile exe; the release script's own in-place/extracted smoke tests for the zip). Full suite re-confirmed green before the build: 416 passing, 1 skipped.

- 2026-08-24: Added an opt-in "Mirror fitting" checkbox to the Ships / Fit workspace's manual weapon-slot editor (`gui/main_window.py`). Assigning or clearing a weapon on one mount also applies it to that mount's detected left/right mirror partner, wrapping the existing `_choose_slot` assign-then-`run_validate_fit` flow without changing its legality/OP validation at all. Pair detection (`_detect_mirror_mount_pairs`) reads each hull's real parsed `.ship` geometry (`weaponSlots`' `locations`/`angle`/`arc`/`size`/`type`/`mount` -- the same raw fields the canvas already plots) and matches a mount only against another whose position/angle are the exact mirror image (x unchanged, y negated, angle negated mod 360) with identical size/type/mount/arc, never a same-side or superficially-similar mount. Deliberately vertical (left/right) only, per live investigation before implementing: Starsector hulls are built bow/stern-asymmetric (distinct nose vs. engine block), so a "horizontal" mirror axis was checked and found structurally meaningless for real ship geometry -- it was dropped rather than shipped as an always-inert third toggle state. Verified against real hulls pulled from the live install: Hammerhead's 8 mounts pair perfectly (4 pairs); Dominator's 16 mostly pair by angle with 2 correctly-unpaired centerline hardpoints; Odyssey's 19 mounts produce exactly 1 real pair, with the rest -- superficially similar but not true mirror images, e.g. two same-type/same-angle turrets whose x/y don't actually negate -- correctly left unpaired rather than false-matched. The checkbox is disabled with a "no symmetric pairs detected" tooltip on a hull with none, and shows a live pair count otherwise, so its usefulness (or lack of it, e.g. on Odyssey) is visible before a user clicks it. Built-in/hardwired mounts are skipped from mirroring. 6 new tests (`tests/test_gui_canvas.py`): pure-function pair detection (symmetric hardpoints, centerline exclusion, false-near-match rejection using an Odyssey-shaped fixture), an end-to-end `_choose_slot` integration test proving both the mirrored-assignment and mirrored-clear cases, a mirror-off control test, and the disabled-checkbox case. Full suite: 429 passing (416 + 13 across this and the two fixes below), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

  Wiring FLUX-adjusted stats (the prior day's scoring work) into this same mirror-aware fit editor, or extending Mirror to the Retrofits workspace (which has no manual per-mount editing UI at all today, unlike Ships/Fit -- confirmed by reading it directly before scoping this work), remain open, distinct follow-ons not attempted here.

- 2026-08-24: Fixed a second, real progress-reporting gap in `core/scanner.py::scan()`, found while investigating a user report that the scan dialog "still shows marquee and not a counter." The prior scan-progress fix (2026-08-23) addressed a *reporting* bug (progress emitted only in one end-of-scan burst); this is a different, *coverage* bug the fix didn't touch: the fingerprint-check loop (hashing every source's files to test cache eligibility, run before any real parsing) only ever emitted progress on a cache HIT -- meaning on a cache MISS, which is every single source on a genuinely first-ever scan, this whole loop reported nothing at all. Measured directly against the real 148-mod install with a deliberately cold cache (a fresh output directory): a 66.4-second gap between the first "PARSING 0/119" event and the next real update, with the progress dialog sitting at a real but motionless 0/119 the entire time -- functionally indistinguishable from the marquee-hang the prior fix was supposed to have already eliminated, and the dominant fraction of a ~85s total scan. Root-caused with direct instrumentation (wrapping `_source_fingerprint`/`_scan_source_fragment` with per-call timing) before touching any code, per this project's evidence-first discipline, rather than guessing.

  Fixed by emitting a new `FINGERPRINTING` progress stage once per source in that loop, hit or miss, with its own `completed/total` counter independent of `PARSING`'s (which still starts fresh at 0 once fingerprinting finishes, unchanged). Re-measured against the real install with the same cold-cache setup: largest gap between any two consecutive progress events dropped from 66.4s to ~1.1s, across 272 total progress events over the full 25.76s scan. `gui/main_window.py::_scan_progressed` needed no changes -- its stage/count formatting was already generic. 3 new tests: a scanner-level regression test proving `FINGERPRINTING` events report a real, monotonically increasing count on a fully cold cache (`tests/test_scanner.py`), plus the existing staged-progress test's expected stage sequence updated to include the new stage.

- 2026-08-24: Fixed a related, also user-reported usability gap: clicking "Discard Scan Results" (now renamed "Cancel Scan" to match its real behavior) left `scan_button` disabled for the *entire remaining duration* of the scan being discarded, since `Scanner` had no interruption mechanism at all -- the background scan simply ran to natural completion (or failure) regardless, and only then did `_scan_finished` re-enable the button. For a scan pointed at a wrong or slow location, this meant a potentially long, unpredictable wait before the user could even retry with a corrected path -- the exact scenario the project owner flagged.

  Added real, bounded cooperative cancellation to `core/scanner.py::Scanner` (`cancel_check: Callable[[], bool] | None`, checked once per source in both the fingerprinting loop and the parsing/executor loop; a new `ScanCancelled` exception). Deliberately cooperative, not preemptive -- Python threads can't be safely killed mid-work, so a source's own fingerprint/parse already in flight still finishes before the scan actually stops, bounding cancellation to roughly one source's worth of time (measured on the real install: cancelling after 20/119 sources fingerprinted stopped the whole scan in 1.33s, versus the old behavior's up to ~85s worst-case wait) rather than being instant. The executor-loop path needed care: futures for every remaining source are submitted upfront, so simply raising and letting `with ThreadPoolExecutor(...)`'s default `__exit__` run would have waited for the *entire remaining queue* regardless -- fixed by managing the executor manually and calling `shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=True)` in a `finally`, which cancels every not-yet-started future outright and waits only for one already in flight.

  Threaded through `api.run_scan` (`cancel_check` param, default `None`, zero behavior change for other callers) and `gui/workers/scan_worker.py::ScanWorker` (gained a real `threading.Event`-backed `cancel_check`, and a new `cancelled` signal so a user-requested cancellation reaches the GUI as a calm status message rather than routing through the same `failed`/`QMessageBox.critical` path a genuine error would -- confirmed by direct unit test that `ScanWorker.run()` emits `cancelled`, not `failed` or `completed`, when its cancel check is set).

  Also added the upfront validation the project owner asked for, addressing the same root complaint from a different angle: `_looks_like_starsector_install()` checks for `starsector-core`/`starsector.exe`/`starsector.sh` before a scan ever starts, since `core/scanner.py::discover_sources` is deliberately lenient -- if `starsector-core` isn't found under the chosen root it silently scans the root itself instead of failing, meaning a wrong path was never rejected on its own and could mean recursively walking an arbitrary, unrelated, possibly enormous folder tree. `_start_scan` now also rejects an empty installation/output path and a non-writable output directory with a clear, specific `QMessageBox.warning` before any background work begins, rather than surfacing a generic exception from deep inside the worker thread. 9 new tests across `tests/test_scanner.py` (cooperative cancellation stopping the scan before completion; a second test specifically exercising the ThreadPoolExecutor-path cancellation and its `shutdown(cancel_futures=True)` cleanup, proving no hang) and `tests/test_gui_canvas.py` (the install-detection helper; `_start_scan` rejecting a non-install path and an empty path without ever creating a scan thread; `_discard_scan` setting the real cancel flag synchronously; `ScanWorker.run()`'s cancelled-vs-failed signal routing). Full suite: 429 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-24: Made `dist/build_voidsmith.bat`'s underlying script (`tools/build_gui_exe.ps1`) self-versioning, per the project owner's request to rebuild "on the fly" without needing the version bumped by hand first. Added `Step-PatchVersion`, run by default before every build (opt out with `-NoVersionBump`): bumps `pyproject.toml`'s patch version and rewrites `uv.lock`'s own recorded version for the local editable `voidsmith` package to match in the same step -- exactly the manual two-file edit this project has had to do by hand for every prior version bump (0.1.0->0.1.1->0.1.2), since `uv sync --locked` refuses to proceed once the two diverge. Deliberately writes both files via `[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText` with an explicit no-BOM UTF8 encoding rather than PowerShell's `Set-Content -Encoding utf8`, which adds a BOM neither file originally has on Windows PowerShell 5.1. Dry-run verified against copies of the real files before ever touching them (correct bump, correct sync, no BOM introduced) before using it for a real build.

  Used it for this session's actual rebuild (Mirror fitting, both scan-progress fixes, and the two deepening tasks below): cleared `dist/`, ran the now-self-bumping `build_voidsmith.bat` (auto-bumped 0.1.2 -> 0.1.3, built `VoidSmith.exe`, 49,119,100 bytes), then `tools/build_portable_release.ps1 -FreshAnalysis` at the now-current 0.1.3 with no further bump needed (`VoidSmith-0.1.3-win-x64.zip`, 41,588,324 bytes, SHA-256 `4f143689...`). Both smoke-tested clean. Full suite green before the build: 429 passing, 1 skipped.

- 2026-08-24: Investigated wiring the FLUX hullmod modeling into `generation/vent_cap.py::allocate_vents_and_capacitors` (the flux-budget vent/capacitor allocation a candidate build's OP goes toward), as the natural next step after scoring's own FLUX wiring. Concluded, with evidence, that it is **not** soundly wireable within the existing cache-wiring boundary, and made no code changes -- a legitimate, deliberately-scoped "no" rather than a forced fit.

  The blocking ordering question was checked and resolved favorably: `generation/candidate.py::_build_candidate` finalizes hullmod selection (`select_hullmods`, line ~374) *before* calling `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` (line ~384), so a candidate's own hullmod choices (e.g. `fluxdistributor`) really are known by the time vent allocation runs -- no circular dependency. The real blocker is a pre-existing, deliberate design choice: `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` takes its own `heuristic_set` parameter, but `_build_candidate` never forwards the caller's real one -- it always calls with the implicit default `"baseline_0.2"`, a choice `candidate.py`'s own comments document explicitly (`CANDIDATE_GENERATION_FIXED_VENT_CAP_HEURISTIC_SET`) specifically so `candidate_alternatives_fingerprint`'s `AnalysisResultCache` context can report an honest, non-varying value. Making vent allocation genuinely hullmod/heuristic-aware (the `baseline_0.8`-style gate this would need) requires `_build_candidate` to forward a real, caller-varying `heuristic_set` down to it -- but the cache fingerprint doesn't hash that as a varying input, so doing so without also touching the fingerprint would let `AnalysisResultCache` serve a stale/wrong vent-cap allocation to a call whose `constraints_hash` happens to collide with an earlier one made under a different `heuristic_set` -- a real correctness bug, not a hypothetical one, since that cache path is genuinely used (`api.py::run_generate`, GUI workers). Also checked whether `generation/refit.py` offered an unreserved path to the same fix -- it doesn't call `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` at all (its own flux-related pass only caps existing vent/capacitor counts down during `FIX_LEGALITY`, never allocates new ones).

  Live-quantified the real, still-open inaccuracy this leaves rather than just asserting it exists: a Hammerhead candidate (base `flux_dissipation=250.0`) carrying `heavyburst`+2x`pdlaser` and `fluxdistributor` currently gets 4 vents + 20 capacitors allocated under BALANCED flux mode, computed against the unmodified 250.0 base; the hullmod-aware 310.0 effective value would allocate 0 vents + 20 capacitors instead -- a real, avoidable 4-vent/4-OP difference the current pipeline can't correct without first threading a real `heuristic_set` through the reserved cache-wiring surface. Independently re-verified: confirmed the call-site ordering, the fixed-default call, and the absent `refit.py` call site directly in the source; full suite re-run, unchanged at this point in the session (no edits were made, matching the report). Left as a documented, scoped-out follow-on rather than forced.

- 2026-08-24: Added a fifth hullmod-effect category, COMBAT (`adapters/vanilla/__init__.py::COMBAT_HULLMOD_EFFECTS`, `analysis/weapon_range_stats.py`), after researching every remaining unmodeled category (EXPLORATION, SALVAGE, SURVEY, SENSOR, CARRIER, CONVERSION, FLEET_SUPPORT) via a `hull_mods.csv` desc-text sweep and finding COMBAT the only one with strong, unconditional, per-hull-size numeric evidence against an already-existing typed field. Two verified hullmods modeled, both cross-checked live against the real install's CSV and the wiki (2026-08-24 fetch): `targetingunit` (Integrated Targeting Unit, +10/20/40/60% `Weapon.range` for BALLISTIC/ENERGY weapons by hull size) and `dedicated_targeting_core` (Dedicated Targeting Core, +35/50% for cruiser/capital only -- frigate/destroyer correctly omitted, matching its own desc text and the live CSV's `cost_frigate=0/cost_dest=0`). Eleven further real candidates were researched and explicitly excluded with cited reasons: several (`advancedoptics`, `high_scatter_amp`) target beam weapons specifically, a sub-category with no typed field (`Weapon.mount_type` only distinguishes BALLISTIC/ENERGY/MISSILE/DECORATIVE, not beam-vs-non-beam); several more (`turretgyros`, `missleracks`, `pointdefenseai`) target stats with no Weapon field at all (turn rate, ammo/RoF, AI targeting behavior); `eccm`/`escort_package` are conditional on real-time battlefield state; four (`advancedcore`, `supercomputer`, `terminator_core`, `distributed_fire_control`) are `hidden=TRUE`, AI-core-granted, never player-installable.

  This is the first hullmod-effect category whose verified effect lives on `Weapon`, not `Hull`: both hullmods are installed on the hull but grant their bonus to each individually *equipped* weapon, so `compute_derived_combat_stats` deliberately takes the whole `Variant` (for `weapons_by_mount`), unlike the four existing categories' `(hull, hullmod_ids, registry)` shape. Same never-fabricate-a-stack discipline as the other four: the two hullmods are mutually illegal in vanilla and both target the same stat (`Weapon.range`) via the same operation shape when they somehow co-occur -- `effective_range_by_mount` stays `None` for that mount with each contribution preserved individually in `applied_effects`, rather than guessing how vanilla would combine two percent bonuses.

  Wired into `svg analyze-variant`'s output via a new `VariantAnalysis.weapon_range_stats` field, auto-serialized through the existing report path. Independently re-verified beyond the agent's own report: read `adapters/vanilla/__init__.py`'s new table and `analysis/weapon_range_stats.py`'s consumer directly, confirmed the `variant.py` wiring, and re-ran the claimed live number against a fresh scan of the real install myself -- the real `apogee_Balanced` variant (CRUISER, carries `targetingunit`) shows `ionbeam` range 1000.0 -> 1400.0 (exactly the cited 40% cruiser bonus), with `stabilizedshieldemitter`/`magazines` correctly excluded from this COMBAT-specific consumer via `unverified_hullmod_ids` since neither is a COMBAT-category hullmod. Stress pass across all 5,526 real registry variants: 0 exceptions, 170 variants with a real applied effect, 0 mount-type violations, 0 real-data stacking collisions (consistent with the two hullmods' mutual vanilla incompatibility). 11 new tests (`tests/test_weapon_range_stats.py`: 10; `tests/test_variant_analysis.py`: 1). Full suite: 440 passing (429 + 11 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

  Explicitly not attempted, per instructions: wiring this into `scoring/candidate_score.py` (a distinct, separate follow-on, matching how FLUX's own scoring-wiring was a separate pass one day after its modeling landed).

- 2026-08-24: Fixed a real, user-reported bug that the earlier scan-progress fixes had not actually closed: `api.run_scan`'s own post-`Scanner.scan()` work (`Registry.from_scan`, `analyze_change_impact`, `update_manifest`, `result.report`) reported zero progress no matter how long it took, even though `Scanner.scan()` itself now reports incrementally throughout. Measured directly on the real 148-mod install: `analyze_change_impact` alone (diffing the full manifest, ~13k+ entities) took a real, silent 4.3s. To a user watching the GUI's progress dialog, the scan reads as stuck again right at the end, after Scanner's own part had genuinely finished -- exactly matching the user's report that 0.1.3 was "still only showing marquee and disabled scan" despite the earlier fix. Added three new post-scan progress events (`BUILDING_REGISTRY`, `ANALYZING_CHANGES`, `WRITING_REPORT`), emitted via the same `progress_callback` Scanner already uses -- not granular counters (none of these three steps have anything countable to report against), but each proves forward motion instead of a frozen dialog. 3 new tests (`tests/test_api_scan_progress.py`, the first test file to exercise `api.run_scan` directly): the three new stages fire in order after `COMPLETE`, each reports real entity/diagnostic counts, and passing no `progress_callback` remains a true no-op. Full suite: 443 passing (440 + 3 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-24: Added incremental drag-and-drop mod incorporation, per a direct request to speed up testing iteration: previously, a dropped mod was only queued for the *next full rescan* ("Added N additional mod(s); rescan to include them"), meaning testing one new mod meant re-scanning the whole 148-mod install every time. Now, if a scan already exists in the session (and neither a real scan nor another incorporation is already running), a drop is incorporated immediately.

  New backend entry points, not a GUI-only trick: `core/scanner.py::Scanner.scan_single_mod(mod_root)` (a public wrapper -- parses exactly one mod directory in isolation, returning its resolved `ModInfo` and parsed fragment, or `None` if it's not a real mod folder) and `api.py::run_incremental_mod_scan(config, existing_result, mod_roots)` (merges one or more freshly-parsed mods into a *copy* of an already-scanned `ScanResult` -- `existing_result` itself is never mutated -- then re-resolves skins against the full merged hull set and rebuilds the `Registry` from the complete merged entity list, so cross-source duplicate-ID detection stays full-fidelity even though nothing outside the dropped mod(s) is re-verified against current disk state). Explicitly documented as not a substitute for a real full scan: a source that changed on disk since the last full scan won't be picked up by this path, and a skin left unresolved by the *original* scan isn't retried here (that working state is scan-instance-local and already discarded).

  `gui/main_window.py::dropEvent` now chooses this path automatically when applicable, falling back to the original "rescan to include" behavior otherwise (nothing scanned yet, or a scan/incorporation is already in flight -- reusing `scan_button`'s enabled state as that guard). The completion handling was factored into a named method, `_apply_incremental_scan_outcome`, specifically so it could be exercised directly and synchronously in tests -- a real background-thread completion timing turned out not to be reliably observable via `processEvents()` polling in this project's headless test harness (confirmed directly: even a trivial, guaranteed-fast existing `_run` operation never completed within 5 seconds / 2.3 million polls in the same setup), independent of whether the real app -- which runs a normal blocking `app.exec()` event loop, not a polling one -- has any actual issue. 6 new tests: 5 in `tests/test_incremental_mod_scan.py` covering the backend directly (a dropped mod is merged without touching the rest; `existing_result` is never mutated; an invalid mod path is skipped, not raised; a dropped mod colliding with an existing hull ID is caught as a real duplicate by the rebuilt registry; multiple mods dropped in one call are all merged), 2 in `tests/test_gui_canvas.py` (`dropEvent` chooses the incremental path and shows the right status message; `_apply_incremental_scan_outcome` correctly refreshes the hull list/catalog/filters from a real merged outcome). Full suite: 460 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-24: Wired COMBAT hullmod range bonuses (`targetingunit`, `dedicated_targeting_core`) into scoring, the same day's natural follow-on to the COMBAT modeling pass, mirroring FLUX's own scoring-wiring exactly. Investigation found the effect belongs in *two* scoring components, not one -- both `range_coherence` (min/max spread across mounted weapons) and `role_match` (per-weapon comparison against `artillery_min_range`/`brawler_max_range` for LONG_RANGE/SHORT_RANGE profiles) read `Weapon.range` directly, so both needed the fix. Released as a new opt-in `baseline_0.9` registry entry (`{**BASELINE_0_8.values, "combat_hullmod_adjustment_enabled": 1.0}`, preserving every earlier baseline byte-for-byte; shipped default stays `baseline_0.7`), gating a new `_combat_adjusted_ranges()` helper that substitutes `compute_derived_combat_stats`'s `effective_range_by_mount` for each mount's raw range when computing both components. Same-stat-stacking handled identically to the FLUX precedent: a mount with 2+ verified hullmods falls back to the weapon's raw base range with an explanatory note, never a fabricated combined value.

  Verified non-trivial on real data, not just plausible: scanned the real 148-mod install for the 2,640 variants carrying either hullmod and diffed every `(variant, profile)` score under `baseline_0.8` vs `baseline_0.9` -- 47 real combinations showed a genuine score delta, including real vanilla core hulls (`venture_Balanced` under LINE_ARTILLERY: `role_match` 70.0 -> 100.0 as heavyac's boosted range crosses the artillery-range threshold; `eagle_d_Assault` similarly). Independently re-verified: confirmed `BASELINE_0_9`'s dict-spread construction and the unchanged `AppConfig` default directly in source. 7 new tests (`tests/test_scoring.py`): 2 byte-identity regression tests for `baseline_0.7`/`baseline_0.8`, 2 proving the new baseline's real component-score changes, 1 for the stacking fallback. `HEURISTICS.md` updated. Full suite: 453 passing at the time this landed (446 + 7 new), 1 skipped, 0 regressions in anything this touched.

- 2026-08-24: Fixed the diagnosed `heuristic_set` plumbing gap that had blocked wiring FLUX effects into `generation/vent_cap.py` earlier today. `generate_conservative_candidate`/`generate_candidate_alternatives`/`_build_candidate` all gained a real `heuristic_set` parameter (default `"baseline_0.2"`, byte-for-byte matching the old hardcoded behavior for every existing caller), forwarded into `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` in place of the fixed constant; `generate_build_archetype_candidates` already had a real `heuristic_set` but was silently dropping it at its own internal `generate_candidate_alternatives` call site (bound to its own named parameter, so it never reached `**generation_options`) -- fixed. `api.py::run_generate` and `run_export` had the identical silent-drop shape in their own kwargs assembly -- both fixed. The now-obsolete `CANDIDATE_GENERATION_FIXED_VENT_CAP_HEURISTIC_SET` constant was removed entirely; `candidate_alternatives_fingerprint`/`build_archetype_candidates_fingerprint` now fold the real, caller-varying `heuristic_set` into their `AnalysisContextFingerprint` instead of a fixed marker, closing the exact cache-staleness risk the earlier investigation flagged.

  Honest finding: this is a correctness/plumbing fix with no currently observable output difference on real data -- every registry entry from `baseline_0.1` through `baseline_0.8` happens to define identical `*_flux_target` values (0.90/0.75/0.55), the only heuristics `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` reads, so `svg generate`'s real output is unchanged today. Live no-op check on the real install (`lasher`/`hammerhead`/`dominator`/`onslaught` across multiple profiles/flux modes, `baseline_0.2` vs `baseline_0.7`) confirmed weapons/hullmods/wings/vents/capacitors/legality all identical. The wiring itself is proven with two fake heuristic-set identifiers carrying deliberately different flux targets (0 vents vs. 5 vents). Independently re-verified: read the real forwarding call site and both fingerprint functions directly in source, confirmed correct; re-ran the specific new cache-staleness regression test myself. 3 new tests in `tests/test_candidate_generation_result_cache.py`, most importantly `test_cached_result_under_one_heuristic_set_is_never_served_to_a_different_heuristic_set` (populates the cache under one heuristic_set, proves a different one forces genuine recomputation rather than serving the stale entry, and that the original entry survives independently afterward). Full suite: 460 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions. FLUX-into-vent_cap itself remains the natural next step, now unblocked, not yet attempted.

- 2026-08-24: Rebuilt both release artifacts as `0.1.4` (the post-scan progress fix, incremental drag-and-drop incorporation, COMBAT-into-scoring `baseline_0.9`, and the vent_cap `heuristic_set` plumbing fix, all above) via the now-self-bumping `build_voidsmith.bat` (auto-bumped 0.1.3 -> 0.1.4) followed by `tools/build_portable_release.ps1 -FreshAnalysis` at the same version. `VoidSmith.exe` (49,262,515 bytes) and `VoidSmith-0.1.4-win-x64.zip` (SHA-256 `589ff784...`), both smoke-tested clean. Full suite green before the build: 460 passing, 1 skipped. This specifically ships the fix for the post-scan silent-progress gap the project owner reported was still present in 0.1.3.

- 2026-08-24: Profiled and fixed the real bottleneck behind `analysis/change_impact.py::analyze_change_impact`'s measured 4.3s cost (found while diagnosing the scan-progress bug earlier today). Profiling first, per this project's established discipline, rather than guessing: the hypothesis that manifest-dict construction/grouping/comparison was the cost turned out wrong (under 20ms at ~14k entities) -- the real cost was entirely in `_direct_impacts`, which re-scanned *every variant* for *every changed weapon/hullmod/fighter/hull*, an O(changed_entities x variants) shape. On a cold first-ever scan (everything ADDED) that's thousands of changed weapons x thousands of variants; cProfile on the real 148-mod install showed 17.9M `dict.values()` calls from the weapons branch alone, 6.98s of an 8.5s cProfile-instrumented run. The warm case (repeat scan, everything UNCHANGED) was already fast (~0.05-0.08s) since the expensive branch never runs when nothing changed -- confirming the fix needed to target the changed-entity path specifically, not the whole function.

  Fixed by building reverse-index dicts (`weapon_id -> [variants]`, etc.) once per call in a single pass over `scan.variants` -- O(variants x mounts + changed_entities) instead of O(changed_entities x variants) -- gated so an index is only built for a category that actually has a change in it. A variant referencing the same id twice (two mounts with the same weapon) can now append to a reverse-index list twice, which is harmless: `add()`'s per-`(kind, target_id, certainty)` set union already collapsed duplicates under the original per-change membership test too, so this preserves identical output, not just similar output. Verified with a golden-output test: captured the *original* implementation's exact serialized output across cold/warm/partial-change scenarios including duplicate-reference edge cases, confirmed the new implementation matches byte-for-byte, then hardcoded that as a permanent regression test (`tests/test_change_impact.py`).

  Measured on the real install (5-run average): cold scan 5.52s -> 1.54s (~3.6x); warm scan 0.08s -> 0.065s (already fast, no meaningful change expected or found). Independently re-verified: read `_direct_impacts`'s new implementation directly, confirmed the reverse-index/idempotency reasoning holds, and re-ran the real 148-mod install myself (1.55s cold, matching the reported range almost exactly). This meaningfully shortens every real first scan on top of today's earlier progress-visibility fixes -- the same phase that used to be a completely silent 4.3s gap is now both visible AND ~3.6x faster. Full suite: 466 passing (460 plus this change's own 1 golden-output regression test, plus several real canonical-hull assertions that ran instead of their usual skip -- `tests/local_fixtures/`/`tests/local_results/` are already populated in this environment from earlier local-benchmark work, unrelated to this change), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-24: Closed out the FLUX-into-vent_cap thread tracked across the last two days: wired `analysis/flux_stats.py::compute_derived_flux_stats`'s verified hullmod-adjusted `flux_dissipation`/`shield_upkeep` into `generation/vent_cap.py::allocate_vents_and_capacitors`'s flux-budget math, now that yesterday's `heuristic_set`-threading fix removed the blocker (vent allocation previously always used a hardcoded value, never the caller's real `heuristic_set`, so an opt-in gate wasn't safely cacheable). Released as a new opt-in `baseline_0.10` (`{**BASELINE_0_9.values, "vent_hullmod_adjustment_enabled": 1.0}`, preserving every earlier baseline byte-for-byte; shipped default stays `baseline_0.7`).

  `allocate_vents_and_capacitors` gained `hullmod_ids`/`registry` parameters; `_build_candidate` passes `hullmod_selection.hullmod_ids` (already finalized before this call runs, the same value that becomes `Variant.hullmods`) and the registry. Same-stat-stacking handled with the identical discipline as every other FLUX consumer: falls back to the raw base value with an explanatory note when 2+ verified hullmods collide, via a `_hullmod_adjusted_flux_stat` helper (a deliberate independent copy of `scoring/candidate_score.py`'s equivalent, since `generation/`/`scoring/` are separate pipeline stages) -- never a fabricated combined value. No changes were needed to the cache fingerprints themselves: they already hash the real `heuristic_set` and the whole hullmod registry, so a `baseline_0.10` result can never be served from a `baseline_0.9`-populated cache entry.

  Independently re-verified beyond the agent's own report: read the new `vent_cap.py` gating logic and the `_build_candidate` call site directly in source, then reproduced the live numbers myself against the real install -- Hammerhead (DESTROYER, base `flux_dissipation=250.0`) carrying 2x `pdburst` + 2x `taclaser` (520 sustained flux/s): `baseline_0.9` allocates 15 vents regardless of `fluxdistributor` (correctly unaffected -- reproducibility preserved), `baseline_0.10` allocates 15 vents without it and exactly 9 with it, matching `compute_derived_flux_stats`'s independently-reported `250.0 -> 310.0` effective value exactly. Full suite: 466 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-24: Found and fixed a third O(n×m) profiling win, continuing the same "measure before optimizing" pass that already fixed `_build_candidate` and `_direct_impacts`.
