- 2026-08-25: Implemented ROADMAP Phase 28 (Local Hullmod/System Interpretation Expansion, Charter Priority 6). Re-confirmed the starting baseline before touching anything: 579 passing, 1 skipped. Read `analysis/hullmod_static_analysis.py`, all six `adapters/vanilla/` effect-category tables, and `core/overrides.py` fully first, per the task's own instruction, to determine which of the charter's 8 evidence tiers already existed before assuming any were missing.

  Found the charter's own suggested starting hypothesis was wrong: tier 3 (a versioned API-effect registry) already existed and was working -- `API_EFFECT_REGISTRY_VERSION = "starsector-api-effects-0.2"` plus `_CALLS`, a table of 14 recognized `MutableShipStatsAPI` accessor methods (`getMaxSpeed`, `getFluxDissipation`, `getArmorBonus`, etc.), matched against `stats.getX().modifyFlat/Percent/Mult(id, value)` call patterns and recording file/line/confidence `EvidenceRecord`s with `EvidenceClass.LOCAL_SOURCE_CODE` -- genuinely tier 2 (local source static analysis) and tier 3 (known-API-call interpretation) already combined into one working analyzer, not a from-scratch design as the charter guessed. Tier 6 (`adapters/`) was also already substantial: six real, wiki-cross-checked vanilla effect tables (LOGISTICS/EFFICIENCY/DEFENSE/MOBILITY/FLUX/COMBAT) covering roughly 20 real hullmods, each with an explicit researched-and-excluded list documenting every hullmod considered and ruled out with a cited reason. Tier 8 (`UNKNOWN_SCRIPTED_EFFECT` as the honest terminal fallback) was already correct.

  The real, well-evidenced gap turned out to be narrower and more concrete than the charter's own guess, found by reading the existing regex closely rather than assuming it was complete: `_MODIFIER`'s value-argument capture group only ever matched a bare numeric literal or a single identifier (`([A-Za-z_][\w]*|[-+]?\d+(?:\.\d+)?)[fFdD]?\)`, anchored immediately before the closing paren) -- a compound arithmetic expression like `1f - (0.01f * RECOIL_BONUS)` or `-HULL_PENALTY` never matched at all, even when every name in the expression was a `static final` constant declared in the very same, already-associated Java file the analyzer was already reading and already extracting single constants from (`_CONSTANT`). Measured this directly against the real local install before deciding it was worth fixing, not assumed: wrote a standalone probe script (kept out of the repo, scratchpad-only) reimplementing the same call-recognition scope as the real analyzer and ran it read-only against all 7,155 real `.java` files under the local 149-mod install's `mods/` directory. First pass (all `stats.getX().modify...` calls regardless of registry membership) overstated the real gain, since most calls target stat accessors the registry never recognized at all (e.g. `getMaxRecoilMult`, `getEmpDamageTakenMult`) -- corrected by filtering to only the 14 already-registered accessors, the real, honest scope of this analyzer's actual coverage: across 2,776 real calls to those 14 accessors, the prior single-token-only logic recognized 1,144 (41%); 1,632 real compound-expression calls were entirely unrecognized and would have fallen to `UNKNOWN_SCRIPTED_EFFECT`.

  Implemented a bounded, safe constant-expression folder in `analysis/hullmod_static_analysis.py`: `_extract_call_arguments` (a proper paren-depth-aware argument splitter, replacing the old single-regex match so the full value expression -- not just one token -- can be captured), `_resolve_modifier_value` (dispatches to the original bare-literal/bare-constant path when the value is simple, or to expression folding otherwise), and `_fold_constant_expression`/`_eval_constant_node` (parses the expression with Python's `ast` module in `mode="eval"` and walks a restricted node whitelist -- `Constant`, `Name`, `UnaryOp` with `+`/`-`, and `BinOp` with `+`/`-`/`*`/`/` only; no calls, attribute access, comparisons, or subscripts are ever evaluated). A `Name` node resolves only against that same file's already-extracted `_CONSTANT` dict; any name that doesn't resolve there -- a method parameter, an instance field, a loop variable, or a constant declared in a genuinely different file -- raises an internal `_UnresolvedExpression` that makes the *whole* expression unresolved rather than partially guessed, identical in spirit to how the original single-token path already refused to guess at a bare unresolvable identifier. Bumped `API_EFFECT_REGISTRY_VERSION` to `starsector-api-effects-0.3`; confirmed this is already wired into `output/analysis_reports.py`'s scan-report cache-invalidation fingerprint (`api_effect_registry` key), so no separate cache-plumbing change was needed for a version bump to correctly invalidate stale cached fragments. Newly recognized (expression-folded) effects are recorded at confidence 0.85, distinct from the original 0.9 for a bare literal/single declared constant -- reflecting that the value is synthesized from combining local constants rather than read directly, never silently merged into the same confidence tier as before.

  Re-measured the real gain with the actual fix in place (same real 149-mod install, same 2,776-call denominator): 491 of the 1,632 previously-unrecognized calls (30% of the gap, an 18-percentage-point / ~43% relative increase in overall real-call recognition) now resolve, across 247 distinct real Java files; the remaining 1,141 correctly stay unresolved because they reference a genuine runtime value. Live-verified end to end through the real, non-reimplemented production function against one real installed hullmod, not just the probe script: the "1130的蔚蓝联邦 translated" mod's real `AF_OpenAmmoDepot` hullmod (`data.hullmods.AF_OpenAmmoDepot`, its real CSV-declared script class, confirmed via that mod's own `hull_mods.csv`) declares `public static final float HULL_PENALTY = 25f;` at line 21 of its real Java source and applies `stats.getHullBonus().modifyPercent(id, -HULL_PENALTY);` at line 36 -- calling `analyze_hullmod_sources` on this real hullmod and real source root previously produced zero recognized effects for this line (a unary-minus expression, not a bare literal or identifier); now it correctly recognizes `hull_hp PERCENT_ADD -25.0` at confidence 0.85, citing the real file and line. (The same mod's `AeCoat` hullmod was also checked directly and confirmed to still produce zero recognized effects even after this fix -- its two compound-expression calls target `getEmpDamageTakenMult`/`getVentRateMult`, neither a registered accessor, correctly outside this analyzer's declared scope rather than a false negative.)

  Confirmed, rather than assumed, that two named tiers remain genuinely unimplemented, documenting both explicitly per the task's own instruction to follow the evidence honestly: tier 4 (referenced local config/constants from a *different* file -- e.g. a mod's own shared constants class or a JSON settings file) has no implementation anywhere in the codebase; the new expression folder deliberately stays same-file-only, proven by a dedicated regression test (`test_does_not_guess_expression_referencing_cross_file_constant`) using a real cross-file pattern this session's install sweep actually found. Tier 7 (explicit manual overrides, `core/overrides.py`) exists but only for `role_tags`/`notes` on weapons and hulls -- AGENTS.md's own "Manual Overrides" section explicitly lists "scripted-effect interpretation when explicitly supplied" as a supported override category, but no `EntityOverride` field or loader logic supports it today. Neither was implemented this phase (the task's own instruction was to pick the single most impactful, well-evidenced gap, not build every remaining tier); both are named here as real, specific follow-on candidates for a future phase rather than left as an unstated gap.

  5 new synthetic-fixture regression tests added to `tests/test_hullmod_static_analysis.py` (neutral invented class/constant names throughout -- `Recoil`/`RECOIL_BONUS`, `Vent`/`VENT_RATE_BONUS`, `Scaled`/`MANEUVER_BONUS`, `Shared`/`CrossRef`/`BONUS`, `Plain`/`SPEED_BONUS` -- no real mod Java source committed, per this project's distribution boundary): two-constant multiplicative folding (`1f - (0.01f * RECOIL_BONUS)`), addition/division folding (`VENT_RATE_BONUS / 100f`), a runtime-parameter expression correctly staying unresolved (`MANEUVER_BONUS * effectLevel`), a cross-file constant reference correctly staying unresolved (proving the same-file-only boundary structurally, via the declared-script-class file-selection mechanism itself, not just via the evaluator), and a regression guard confirming the original bare-literal/single-constant path keeps its exact prior 0.9 confidence unchanged by the new path added alongside it. All 10 tests in the file (5 pre-existing + 5 new) pass. Updated `tests/test_scan_analysis_reports.py`'s one hardcoded `"starsector-api-effects-0.2"` assertion to `-0.3`, and `DATA_SCHEMA.md`'s section 10A registry description to document both confidence sub-tiers and the same-file-only boundary.

  Constraints honored: touched only `src/starsector_variant_generator/analysis/hullmod_static_analysis.py`, `tests/test_hullmod_static_analysis.py`, `tests/test_scan_analysis_reports.py` (one assertion only), `DATA_SCHEMA.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and this log; did not touch `gui/`, `pyproject.toml`'s version, any build/release script, `core/cache.py`, `core/result_cache.py`, `analysis/change_impact.py`, `analysis/complex_hull_audit.py`, `analysis/composite_hulls.py`, `analysis/calibration.py`, `analysis/calibration_runner.py`, `tools/*calibration*.py`, or `analysis/gap_recommendation.py`. Never executed, imported, or otherwise dynamically evaluated any mod's Java source at any point, including during the real-install measurement passes -- static text/regex scanning plus a restricted-grammar `ast.parse`/AST-walk over already-extracted expression *text* only, the same discipline the pre-existing analyzer already used. No git operations were performed (git reported inaccessible due to dubious ownership from a separate concurrent process in this environment, as expected, not investigated further). `ROADMAP.md` Phase 28 marked `COMPLETE_WITH_LIMITATIONS`: the charter's own suggested tier (a versioned API-effect registry) was found already built, and this phase's real contribution is a substantial, real-data-measured expansion of that registry's value-resolution power within its existing same-file-only scope; tier 4 and the scripted-effect-interpretation half of tier 7 remain genuinely unimplemented and are named above for future work rather than silently left as an unstated gap. 5 new tests. Full suite: 584 passing (579 + 5), 1 skipped, 0 regressions -- re-confirmed the 579-passing/1-skipped starting baseline before any edit, matching this task's own stated figure exactly.

- 2026-08-25: Implemented ROADMAP Phase 31 (Scenario-Aware Recommendations, Charter Priority 9). Re-confirmed the true starting baseline before touching anything -- 584 passing, 1 skipped (the task's own stated 579 figure was already stale, superseded by concurrent Phase 28 work landing in this shared repository earlier this session; see the entry directly above). Read `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` fully first, per the task's own instruction -- its `CapabilityGap`/`NativeRecommendation`/`RetrofitRecommendation`/`AcquisitionRecommendation` records, `recommend_native_solutions`/`recommend_retrofit_solutions`/`recommend_acquisition_solutions`, `_rank_build_candidates_for_role`/`_diverse_build_shortlist`, and the `explain_native_candidate`/`explain_build_candidate` Why-Not machinery -- plus `analysis/build_archetypes.py`, `analysis/mechanical_archetypes.py`, and `analysis/capability_vector.py` before designing anything new.

  That read surfaced a real naming collision worth resolving up front: `analysis/scenario_objectives.py::ScenarioObjective` already exists (landed 2026-08-23) and is already imported by `build_archetypes.py`/`generation/candidate.py` -- but it's an unrelated, earlier concept (which *generation-time coverage objectives*, e.g. `LINE_HOLD`/`BREAKTHROUGH`, a single build archetype already supports, each tagged `SUPPORTED`/`PARTIAL`/`UNSUPPORTED`). The charter's new ask is different: a *faction-recommendation* scenario concept (raiding/defense/escort/patrol). Named the new type `ScenarioCategory` specifically to avoid colliding with the existing, actively-imported `ScenarioObjective` class, and documented the distinction in both the code and `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md`.

  Checked for real grounding before inventing a taxonomy, per the task's own instruction: no parseable Starsector hull/variant/faction field anywhere in this project's schema records a documented "mission role," "deployment points," or comparable in-game scenario tag (`DATA_SCHEMA.md` confirmed; the closest real data, the hull CSV `hints` column, covers civilian/logistics roles only). `ScenarioCategory` (`RAIDING`, `DEFENSE`, `ESCORT`, `PATROL`) is therefore kept deliberately small and explicitly labeled in its own docstring as a first-pass heuristic taxonomy, never a documented game mechanic -- the same honest status this module's other first-pass values (`gap_strong_threshold`, etc.) already carry.

  Implemented entirely additively in `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` (the task's required scope, no restructuring of the existing native/retrofit/acquisition ranking logic -- verified by the full pre-existing `tests/test_gap_recommendation.py`/`tests/test_gap_recommendation_result_cache.py` suites passing byte-for-byte unmodified): `recommend_scenario_solutions(faction, registry, gap_result, scenario, heuristic_set, roles=None)` takes an already-computed `GapRecommendationResult` and layers a heuristic `scenario_fit_score` (`_scenario_fit_score`, 0.0-1.0) on top of the Native/Retrofit/Acquisition legs' own already-ranked `hull_id`/`build_archetype_id`/`recommendation_score`/`confidence`, read directly (never recomputed or reordered). `recommend_gap_solutions` itself is completely unmodified -- nothing calls the new function automatically; a caller opts in explicitly with an existing result. Every produced record (`ScenarioRecommendation`) carries `kind = SCENARIO_RECOMMENDATION_KIND` (`"INFERRED_SCENARIO_OPTION"`, matching the charter's exact wording), `source_leg` (which real leg's candidate it reused), and `base_recommendation_score` (that leg's own unmodified score) -- structurally distinguishable from direct evidence, not just labeled that way in prose. `scenario_fit_score` is a fixed weighted combination of already-real, already-computed signals only (`MechanicalArchetypeProfile.compatibility_scores` plus the specific `BuildArchetypeProfile`'s own `tactical_style`/`target_range`/`flux_posture`/`survivability_posture`/`equipment_priorities`); the combination weights are heuristic, the inputs are not.

  Two new heuristics in `core/heuristics.py`'s new, additive `baseline_0.11` (built on `baseline_0.10`, changing nothing for any earlier registry entry): `scenario_fit_min_signal` (default 0.30 -- a candidate below this is simply absent from the result, never padded in, mirroring `unaddressed_gaps`' own "no fabricated recommendation" discipline) and `scenario_confidence_cap` (default 0.75 -- hard-caps every `ScenarioRecommendation.confidence` below the underlying leg's own confidence via `min()`, so a heuristic overlay can never present itself as fully certain, per AGENTS.md's "high score with low confidence must remain visibly low confidence"); plus `scenario_recommendation_count` (default 3.0, a dedicated shortlist-size counterpart to `gap_recommendation_count`). All three are read via `.get()` with an explicit fallback, so the function also works correctly under every earlier heuristic_set that predates `baseline_0.11` -- verified directly, since every new test exercises it under `baseline_0.4`.

  Why-Not: `explain_scenario_candidate` answers "why wasn't `<hull_id>`/`<build_archetype_id>` given as an `INFERRED_SCENARIO_OPTION` for `<scenario>` on `<role>`?" using the exact same real ranking `recommend_scenario_solutions` computes -- never a second inference mechanism, the same discipline every other Why-Not function in this module already follows. Its result (`ScenarioWhyNotExplanation`) always carries the underlying, direct evidence-based `BuildWhyNotExplanation` (`explain_build_candidate`) as a separate `underlying` field, and its own `reason` always states plainly this is a heuristic overlay, never conflating the two into one claim.

  Hand-verified a synthetic fixture -- a 3-medium-ballistic-mount hull with every optional raw stat deliberately left unset, so every mechanical-archetype score is exactly hand-computable from the plain weighted formulas already in `analysis/mechanical_archetypes.py` -- to let the new tests assert exact `scenario_fit_score` values rather than only loose bounds: `DEFENSE` fit for the `LINE_ANCHOR` build derives to exactly 0.511, `RAIDING` fit for the same build to exactly 0.145 (correctly excluded, below the 0.30 signal threshold), while `RAIDING` fit for a *different* build (`FINISHER`) on the *identical* hull derives to 0.495 (correctly included) -- proving exclusion operates per `Hull + BuildArchetype` unit, not per hull, since the native leg's own build-diversity selection can and does surface the same hull under multiple distinct builds. 14 new tests in `tests/test_scenario_recommendation.py` prove: correct `INFERRED_SCENARIO_OPTION` labeling; a low-fit build excluded while a sibling build of the same hull still appears; a scenario with no fitting build at all fully absent; computing scenario recommendations never mutates or changes the content of the underlying `GapRecommendationResult` (asserted via equality before/after two separate scenario calls); confidence always strictly bounded below full certainty even when the underlying leg is fully confident (a separate full-stats fixture engineered so the underlying leg's own confidence is genuinely 1.0, then confirming the 0.75 cap still applies); a `RETROFIT`-sourced scenario option correctly citing its real source `variant_id`; and Why-Not distinguishing "recommended," "considered but below the signal threshold," and "never entered any leg at all" as three different, honestly-labeled facts. Also added a new section 18 to `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md` and a new `ScenarioRecommendation` entry (section 39) to `DATA_SCHEMA.md`, explicitly cross-referencing and distinguishing this from the unrelated `ScenarioObjective` (`DATA_SCHEMA.md` section 21B).

  Constraints honored: touched only `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` (additive only), `core/heuristics.py` (a new, additive `baseline_0.11` -- every earlier baseline's `.values` dict is unchanged), `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md`, `DATA_SCHEMA.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, this log, and the new test file; did not touch `gui/`, `pyproject.toml`'s version, any build/release script, `core/cache.py`, `core/result_cache.py`, `analysis/change_impact.py`, `analysis/complex_hull_audit.py`, `analysis/composite_hulls.py`, `analysis/calibration.py`, `analysis/calibration_runner.py`, `tools/*calibration*.py`, or `core/overrides.py`; read `analysis/build_archetypes.py`/`analysis/mechanical_archetypes.py`/`analysis/capability_vector.py` but made no changes there (no genuine addition was needed). No git operations were performed (git reported inaccessible due to dubious ownership from a separate concurrent process in this environment, as expected). `ROADMAP.md` Phase 31 marked `COMPLETE_WITH_LIMITATIONS`: the new `INFERRED_SCENARIO_OPTION` category is real, tested, and cleanly separated from the mature ranking, but the scenario-fit formula is a genuinely first-pass heuristic (like several other thresholds this engine already carries honestly as first-pass) rather than a benchmarked one, and it is not yet wired into `api.py`/the CLI/GUI -- the task's own concrete scope was the analysis-layer function plus tests, not end-to-end surface wiring, so that remains explicit future work rather than a silent gap. 14 new tests.   Closed it: added `confidence: float | None = None` to all three explanation types (a plain additive frozen-dataclass field, `asdict()`-serialized automatically in the CLI's existing `why-not` JSON report with no further code change needed). Computing it correctly required a new small helper, `_capability_gap_confidence_inputs(profile, role, heuristic_set)`, because Why-Not can legitimately be asked about any role -- including one that is not currently a detected WEAK/GAP-tier gap for the faction -- while every ranking function only ever iterates `detect_capability_gaps`'s tier-filtered output and so only ever has a real `CapabilityGap` object in scope for a role that *is* currently a gap. The new helper mirrors `detect_capability_gaps`'s own per-role `evidence_confidence`/`vector_confidence` formula exactly rather than reinventing it, sharing one hoisted `_ROLE_CAPABILITY_DIMENSION` module constant with `detect_capability_gaps` itself (previously an inline dict local to that one function) so the two computations can never drift apart from two independently-maintained copies of the same table. The existing `_recommendation_confidence(gap, build, extra)` helper every ranking function already calls was refactored to delegate to a new raw-values sibling, `_recommendation_confidence_from_values(evidence_confidence, vector_confidence, build, extra)` -- zero call-site or behavioral change at any of its three existing call sites, confirmed by the full pre-existing suite passing byte-for-byte unmodified before any new assertion was added anywhere. Wired into all three `explain_*_candidate` functions using the exact same audit-trail entry (`best_hull_audit` for native, `best_audit` for retrofit/acquisition) each function already resolves for its rank/score/reason, so the cited confidence is provably tied to the same `RecommendationAudit` entry's `extra["build"]` the real ranking used for that exact candidate -- never a second, independent lookup.

  Added the identity assertions themselves to the three existing consistency tests. Doing so surfaced a real test-authoring gap, not a production bug: `AcquisitionAuditConsistencyTests` had been feeding `recommend_acquisition_solutions` a hand-built `CapabilityGap(..., evidence_confidence=1.0)` directly, rather than a gap actually derived from `analyze_faction_capability`/`detect_capability_gaps` the way the sibling native and retrofit tests already did -- a synthetic gap the real `recommend_gap_solutions` pipeline would never itself construct, since every real caller only ever passes gaps `detect_capability_gaps` produced. Running the new assertion against the old fixture setup caught this immediately (`0.95 != 0.75`) -- not a ranking/Why-Not disagreement, but a test whose own gap input didn't match reality, so it could never have proven anything about confidence regardless of whether production was correct. Fixed by deriving the real gap the same way the other two test classes do; the assertion then passes because both paths are genuinely fed the same real evidence.

- 2026-08-25: Fixed a real, user-flagged gap in `gui/main_window.py::closeEvent`: it had no guard at all against closing while a background `QThread` (the scan, or any generic `_run()`-launched operation tracked in `self._threads`) was still live -- letting Qt tear the window down mid-operation risks the exact same class of race this session already confirmed can segfault PySide6 elsewhere (`QProgressDialog.deleteLater()` racing a still-running thread). Added `self._closing_requested` (set once the user asks to close while work is in flight): `closeEvent` now `event.ignore()`s, requests cooperative cancellation of any in-flight scan (`_discard_scan`), and defers the real close to a new `_maybe_close_after_background_work` helper called from every terminal thread path (`_scan_finished`, `_finish_thread`), which only actually closes once both `self._scan_thread is None` and `self._threads` is empty. Full suite: 608 passing, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-25: Fixed a real, user-flagged waste: `gui/workers/scan_worker.py`'s `ScanWorker.run()` called `api.run_scan(...)` with the default `include_entities=True`, building a full `asdict()` copy of every scanned entity into the returned report -- confirmed by direct grep that `main_window.py` never reads `outcome.report` at all (it works entirely off `outcome.result`/`outcome.registry`), so this was pure waste on every single scan. Fixed by passing `include_entities=False`. Measured directly against the real 148-mod install with a warm scan cache on both sides for a fair comparison (an initial cold-vs-warm comparison was misleading, ~93s vs ~7s, almost entirely cache-state noise, not the flag): warm-to-warm the real difference is modest (~6.99s vs ~6.82s), but the flag still avoids permanently holding a large, entirely-unused duplicate-entity dict in memory for the life of the GUI session.

- 2026-08-25: Fixed a second real, user-flagged issue in the same area: `profiles/modes.py::ModeDefaults.heuristic_set` was hardcoded `"baseline_0.2"` for every mode (Beginner/Guided/Advanced) and never actually read anywhere in the real call chain -- confirmed by grep: every real caller (`api.py`, `cli/main.py`) uses the session's real `AppConfig.heuristic_set` instead. Removed the field entirely (a stale, never-consulted value that still looked authoritative was judged worse than no field -- there's no real "current" value it could hold) rather than leave it as misleading metadata a future caller might reasonably assume reflects the active session heuristic set. Also introduced `core/config.py::DEFAULT_HEURISTIC_SET = "baseline_0.7"` as the single source of truth for "no more specific configuration given," replacing the literal `"baseline_0.7"` duplicated across `AppConfig`'s own field default, its TOML-loading fallback, and 7 identical GUI fallback expressions (`self._config.heuristic_set if self._config is not None else "baseline_0.7"`) with one shared constant. Full suite: 608 passing, 0 regressions.

  Confirmed the real local install path first (`C:\Program Files (x86)\Fractal Softworks\Starsector`, discovered via a full-`C:`-drive search after the configured `config.example.toml` path didn't exist in this environment; 149 mods, 7,155 real `.java` files under `mods/`) before measuring anything, matching this project's own standing "measure before changing" discipline.

  **(1) Registry coverage.** Grepped every real `stats.get<Accessor>().modify(Flat|Percent|Mult)(` call site across the whole local install (not filtered to already-registered accessors this time, to actually find gaps) and tallied by accessor name. 15 further accessors appear at real, non-trivial frequency beyond the existing 14: `getBallisticRoFMult` (212 real calls), `getEnergyWeaponRangeBonus` (165), `getEnergyRoFMult` (160), `getBallisticWeaponRangeBonus` (146), `getShieldDamageTakenMult` (152), `getArmorDamageTakenMult` (135), `getHullDamageTakenMult` (124), `getEmpDamageTakenMult` (118), `getPeakCRDuration` (85), `getMissileRoFMult` (73), `getVentRateMult` (54), `getZeroFluxSpeedBoost` (50), `getMaxCombatReadiness` (48), `getSensorProfile` (38), `getRecoilPerShotMult` (36). Verified real, documented semantics for each before adding one (never guessed): fetched the official Starfarer API's `MutableShipStatsAPI` interface page (`fractalsoftworks.com/starfarer.api`, confirming real existence and `MutableStat`/`StatBonus` return type for every candidate) and a maintained third-party Starsector hullmod-modding guide (`github.com/operator-damexius/Starsector-Modding-Guide-Creating-Custom-Hullmods`) that gives an explicit one-line description for several directly (e.g. `getBallisticWeaponRangeBonus`: "ballistic weapon engagement distance modifier"; `getShieldDamageTakenMult`: "Shield Damage Taken (-20% damage taken) multiplier"; `getZeroFluxSpeedBoost`: "speed advantage when not actively firing weapons"). `getMaxCombatReadiness` was additionally confirmed directly against real vanilla source -- the decompiled `Automated.java` (`com.fs.starfarer.api.impl.hullmods.Automated`, mirrored at `jaghaimo.github.io/starsector-api`) contains `stats.getMaxCombatReadiness().modifyFlat(id, -MAX_CR_PENALTY, "Automated ship penalty")`, the strongest evidence tier available (real vanilla code, not just an interface signature). The remaining rate-of-fire/damage-taken siblings (`getEnergyRoFMult`/`getMissileRoFMult` alongside the guide-confirmed `getBallisticRoFMult`; `getArmorDamageTakenMult`/`getHullDamageTakenMult`/`getEmpDamageTakenMult` alongside the guide-confirmed `getShieldDamageTakenMult`) were accepted on the same basis the existing registry's own `getBallisticWeaponFluxCostMod`/`getEnergyWeaponFluxCostMod`/`getMissileWeaponFluxCostMod` triple already establishes: confirmed real via the official API surface, semantically unambiguous from a parallel, self-descriptive method name within an already-verified family. All 15 added to `_CALLS`; `API_EFFECT_REGISTRY_VERSION` bumped `starsector-api-effects-0.3` -> `-0.4` (already wired into the scan-report cache-invalidation fingerprint per Phase 28, no separate plumbing needed).

  Measured through the real production function (`analyze_hullmod_sources`, the actual per-hullmod `DECLARED_SCRIPT_CLASS`/`ID_REFERENCE_FALLBACK` association path, not a reimplementation) against the same 2,180 real scanned hullmods (enabled + disabled): recognized effects rose 742 -> 1,424 (+682, ~92%), hullmods with >=1 recognized effect rose 310 -> 499 (+189). Reported this narrower, production-scoped figure rather than a larger raw whole-install call-site grep count (6,854 total real matches across all accessors, recognized or not; 1,596 raw matches for the 15 newly-registered accessors specifically) -- the production number is what actually reaches `hullmod_source_analysis.json` for a real user, so it's the honest one to lead with.

  **(2) Cross-class constant resolution.** Phase 28 explicitly left this open as tier 4 ("referenced local config/constants"): the existing folder only resolved a `static final` constant declared in the *same* `.java` file as the call site. Implemented `_cross_file_constants(source_root)`, a same-source-root (never cross-mod), per-file-stem-as-class-name lookup table built once per scan from the already-indexed `_java_sources` listing (no second file-read pass), and extended `_eval_constant_node`'s restricted AST grammar with one new node type (`ast.Attribute`, a `ClassName.CONSTANT`-shaped qualified reference -- the task's own cited example, `Constants.HULL_PENALTY`), resolved only against this table. The mod-boundary safety property is structural, not a runtime check: the table is built exclusively from `_java_sources(source_root)`, itself already scoped to one mod's (or the core game's) own `.java` tree by the caller, so a different mod's class can never be reached -- proven by a dedicated regression test using a qualifier naming a class this source root never declares, confirming the lookup fails closed. A resolution touching this table is recorded at a new, lower confidence tier (0.75) than either same-file sub-tier (0.9 bare, 0.85 same-file expression), reflecting the added uncertainty of the file-stem-as-class-name assumption -- reusing, not inventing, the same simplification `DECLARED_SCRIPT_CLASS` association already relies on. Deliberately stays qualified-reference-only: a bare name resolved only via a Java `import static` remains unimplemented (a dedicated regression test confirms the pre-existing bare-cross-file-reference case still correctly stays unresolved, unchanged by this addition).

  Measured in isolation against the real install via a dedicated before/after script (restricting the registry to the original 14 accessors while keeping cross-file resolution active, comparing against the same 14-only/same-file-only baseline): 0 additional real resolutions for the original 14 accessors (no real hullmod in this install references a cross-file qualified constant through one of them), and exactly 4 real effects specifically through the newly-added `-0.4` accessors that would otherwise have stayed unresolved. Reported as measured -- a small, real, honestly-stated number, not inflated to look more impactful than the real data supports; genuinely rare in this install's own real call sites to the registered accessor family is itself real evidence.

  **(3) Explicit `UNSUPPORTED_SCRIPTED_EFFECT` vs `UNKNOWN_SCRIPTED_EFFECT`.** Added `unsupported_scripted_portions: tuple[str, ...] = ()` to `HullmodStaticAnalysis` (additive, defaulted, so every existing positional-argument construction in the module keeps working unchanged), populated only when a call site already matched a real, registered `_CALLS` accessor but its value argument could not resolve -- a runtime variable, a cross-mod reference, an unfoldable expression, or an argument list the deliberately single-line parser couldn't extract at all -- reclassified out of the now-narrower `unknown_scripted_portions`, reserved for a call/reference the registry has no entry for whatsoever (or a structural no-source/no-class state). Purely a re-labeling of which branch an already-computed unresolved case fell into, per the task's own explicit constraint -- no new inference, no value ever synthesized to dodge the label. The confidence formula and the "no recognized modifier at all" terminal fallback were both updated to treat `unsupported` the same as `unknown` for the partial-vs-full-understanding distinction, so a class with real effects plus any unresolved remainder (of either kind) still lands at the pre-existing 0.6 partial-confidence tier rather than being silently upgraded just because the remainder is now split across two fields.

  Measured against the real install: 477 real `UNSUPPORTED` portions (registry-recognized, value-unresolvable) across 164 real hullmods, now distinguishable from 6,318 real `UNKNOWN` portions -- previously an undifferentiated combined total of 7,477 before this phase's own registry expansion moved part of that total into genuinely resolved effects instead.

Updated `tests/test_scan_analysis_reports.py`'s hardcoded registry-version assertion (`-0.3` -> `-0.4`) and `DATA_SCHEMA.md`'s section 10A to describe the new confidence tier, the expanded accessor count (14 -> 29), and the two-field unresolved-remainder split.

  Constraints honored: touched only `analysis/hullmod_static_analysis.py`, its own tests, `tests/test_scan_analysis_reports.py`'s one hardcoded version-string assertion, `DATA_SCHEMA.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and this log; read `adapters/vanilla/__init__.py` fully (for its established wiki-citation rigor/format) but made no changes there -- this phase's registry additions are `MutableShipStatsAPI` accessor-method semantics, a different evidence class from `adapters/vanilla`'s per-hullmod balance-value citations, and needed no new adapter table entries. Did not touch `gui/`, `pyproject.toml`'s version, any build/release script, `core/scanner.py`, `core/cache.py`, `core/result_cache.py`, `output/analysis_reports.py`, `analysis/calibration.py`, `analysis/calibration_runner.py`, `analysis/gap_recommendation.py`, `analysis/composite_hulls.py`, `analysis/change_impact.py`, or `core/overrides.py`. No git operations were performed (git reported inaccessible due to dubious ownership from a separate concurrent process in this environment, as expected). `ROADMAP.md` Phase 38 marked `COMPLETE_WITH_LIMITATIONS`: all three closed items are real, measured, and regression-tested against the real local install, but this is a genuine partial pass by the task's own explicit permission -- local config/CSV reference resolution, richer system-effect interpretation, and partial per-method extraction from mixed known/unknown scripts remain unimplemented and are named here as real follow-on candidates, not silently left as an unstated gap. 7 new tests. Full suite: 616 passing (608 confirmed starting baseline + this phase's own 7 + 1 from concurrent Phase 37 scan-performance work landing in `tests/test_scanner.py` this same session, confirmed via file-modification timestamps rather than assumed, outside this phase's own file scope), 1 skipped, 0 regressions.

- 2026-08-25 (ROADMAP Phase 37, user Phase 1, Scan Performance): profiled before changing anything, per this phase's own instruction, and confirmed the 608-passing/1-skipped baseline first. The worker-count benchmark (1/2/4/8 against the real 149-mod install, cold cache, 2 runs each) had already been run earlier this session: workers=1 median 22.6s (min 21.98s), workers=2 median 21.3s (min 21.03s), workers=4 median 22.4s (min 21.47s), workers=8 median 24.9s (min 24.64s) -- confirming the existing historical finding (2026-08-23's "Measured source-snapshot optimization" entry, ~18s serial vs ~18.6s two-worker vs ~19.8s eight-worker): this workload is disk-I/O-bound, not CPU-bound, so `Scanner.max_workers` (which gates PARSING) stayed serial by default; not re-measured again this pass.

  Worked through the rest of the phase's scope in order:

  1. **Parallelized fingerprinting.** `core/scanner.py::Scanner.scan()`'s fingerprint-check loop (hashing each source's files to decide cache eligibility) was serial, unlike PARSING's already-threaded stage. Given PARSING's own serial-is-optimal finding, measured this independently rather than assuming the same conclusion applied: an isolated microbenchmark (calling `Scanner._source_fingerprint` directly against every real discovered source, fresh hash cache per attempt, correctness-checked -- parallel output byte-identical to serial) found serial median 8.67s (min 8.46s) across 3 runs; 2 workers 6.70s; 3 workers 5.99s; 4 workers median 5.53s (min 5.23s, the consistent best across 5 total runs at that count); 6 and 8 workers both measured slightly worse than 4. Confirmed with a second, independent measurement directly on the real `Scanner.scan()` path itself (`tools/profile_scan.py`, same-harness A/B, temporarily forcing the new pool to 1 worker for the "before" figure, 3 runs each): warm-cache total dropped from median 5.89s (fingerprinting alone 5.78s) to median 3.84s (fingerprinting alone 3.68s), ~35%; a cold run improved too, 20.72s -> 17.56s (smaller relative gain since PARSING still dominates a cold scan). Implemented as a new bounded pool (`Scanner._source_fingerprint_isolated`, capped at 4 workers, sized independently of `max_workers` since this is a distinct, separately-measured question from the already-decided PARSING default) dispatched up front so real hashing runs concurrently in the background; the coordinator still walks `sources` in fixed index order and blocks on each index's own future in turn, so FINGERPRINTING progress events keep the exact same deterministic per-source order/cadence the existing tests already assert on -- no progress-event test needed to change for this part. Each isolated job merges its own `_hash_cache`/`_hash_bytes` back into the coordinator's totals so later stages (PARSING's own workers, seeded from the coordinator's hash cache) still see every file this pass already hashed rather than re-reading it from disk. New test: `test_source_fingerprint_isolated_matches_serial_and_reports_hash_state_to_merge`.

  2. **Switched parse-future harvesting to `as_completed()`.** The PARSING stage's harvest loop used to block on `futures[index].result()` in submission order -- under a caller-supplied `max_workers > 1`, a later-submitted-but-faster source's progress report and cache write were delayed behind whichever earlier-submitted source happened to still be running, even after the faster one had genuinely finished. Switched to `concurrent.futures.as_completed(future_to_index)`, reporting/persisting each source the moment it's genuinely done in true completion order; `parsed_fragments` stays keyed by source index, and the separate merge loop after the harvest block (unchanged) still walks `sources` in fixed index order regardless of harvest order, so Registry construction and report output stay exactly as reproducible as before. This also meant PARSING now emits one progress event per source (on completion) instead of a "starting" + "done" pair, since `as_completed()` only yields a future once it has already resolved -- a pre-wait event no longer reflects anything real. Updated `test_scan_emits_staged_progress_and_records_workload_metrics` to the new, intentional cadence and added a new regression test, `test_parse_futures_harvest_in_completion_order_but_merge_stays_deterministic`, that deliberately makes `core` (submitted first) the slowest source via a `Scanner` subclass and asserts both (a) PARSING progress genuinely reports `fixture_mod` (submitted second, finishes first) before `core`, and (b) the merged report is byte-identical to a plain single-worker, submission-order scan of the same fixtures.

  3. **Warm-start behavior.** The fingerprinting parallelization above turned out to *be* the real warm-start lever: on a warm run (cache hit on every source) PARSING's own `source_parsing` stage now measures ~0s, and fingerprinting is essentially the entire remaining cost -- exactly what got cut ~35% in step 1. Looked for a cheaper staleness pre-check (mtime/size before a full hash) beyond that, per the task's own suggestion, but declined it: `Scanner` is deliberately stateless across process invocations (no persistent DB, per this project's own "everything read fresh per command" architecture), so an mtime/size skip would need a new persistent stat-cache artifact with its own invalidation story, and would weaken the real content-hash staleness guarantee for an unmeasured, likely-small additional gain -- SHA-256 hashing itself is already cheap (C-accelerated); the dominant real cost is the disk read a stat call doesn't avoid. Not worth the correctness risk against this phase's own explicit "never weaken a real staleness guarantee" constraint, so left as a documented, deliberately-not-taken option rather than invented scope.

  4. **Dependency-complete selective cache reuse.** Re-read `output/analysis_reports.py::write_scan_analysis_reports`/`reuse_if_unchanged` fully rather than assuming a gap existed. Found it already dependency-complete beyond the top-level full-manifest fast path: independent per-hull (`_hull_profile_fingerprint`), per-faction (`_faction_capability_fingerprint`/`_faction_doctrine_fingerprint`), per-source-mod weapon/hullmod classification (`_write_equipment_fragments`), and per-source-mod hullmod Java-analysis (`_write_hullmod_source_fragments`) fragment reuse all already exist from Phase 2. The one coarser boundary found -- weapon/hullmod classification fragments key on a whole source mod's entities, not one entity at a time, so one changed weapon recomputes classification for every weapon in that mod -- is a deliberate, already-established pattern shared with the Java-analysis fragments, and classification itself is a cheap, pure function (unlike the Java static analysis that pattern also bounds), so finer per-entity granularity there would add real complexity for no meaningful measured win. Reported honestly as "no worthwhile gap found" rather than inventing new scope to fill this line item.

  5. **Timing/cache-hit visibility.** `ScanMetrics.stage_seconds` gained two new, additive keys: `fingerprinting` (the fingerprint-check loop alone) and `source_parsing` (the PARSING executor block alone) -- the existing combined `parsing` key is unchanged, still the sum of both plus the final merge, so no existing caller (including `gui/main_window.py`, which only reads `stage_seconds["total"]`) sees any different value for a key it already read. Added `ScanMetrics.cache_hit_rate` (`sources_reused / sources_scanned`, `0.0` when nothing was scanned), computed once in `Scanner.scan()` rather than requiring every caller to divide the two existing counts itself; surfaced in `tools/profile_scan.py`'s JSON output. Read (read-only, per this phase's constraint) `gui/main_window.py::_scan_progressed`: confirmed the GUI's live progress dialog shows stage/count/entities/current-source but never cache-hit information, and its post-scan "Performance:" summary line reports `sources_scanned`/`files_hashed`/total but not `sources_reused`/`cache_hit_rate` -- a real, found gap, left unfixed since this phase may not touch `gui/`.

  Added/updated 6 tests in `tests/test_scanner.py` (2 new: the completion-order/determinism regression and the isolated-fingerprint correctness test; 4 updated: the staged-progress test's new cadence plus stage-seconds assertions, and `cache_hit_rate` assertions on the existing full-reuse and partial-reuse tests). Ran the relevant suite (`tests.test_scanner`, `tests.test_api_scan_progress`) and the full suite throughout; final full-suite run: 617 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions from this phase's own changes -- the count moved up from the session's confirmed 608 baseline because an unrelated concurrent process (git reported inaccessible due to dubious ownership from that same separate process, as already documented elsewhere in this file) independently landed Phase 38's own new tests in `analysis/hullmod_static_analysis.py`/`tests/test_hullmod_static_analysis.py`/`tests/test_scan_analysis_reports.py` during this same session; confirmed via file mtimes that none of this phase's own scope files (`core/scanner.py`, `core/models.py`, `output/analysis_reports.py`, `tools/profile_scan.py`) were touched by that other process. No git operations performed, per this phase's own constraint (also expected-inaccessible for the same reason). `ROADMAP.md` Phase 37 marked `COMPLETE_WITH_LIMITATIONS`: every scoped item was addressed (measured and kept, measured and declined, or reviewed and found already sufficient) with two honestly reported limitations -- no mtime/size staleness pre-check, and the GUI's live progress dialog still doesn't surface cache-hit information.

- 2026-08-25 (ROADMAP Phase 40, user Phase 4, Scenario Objective Expansion): confirmed the 617-passing/1-skipped starting baseline before touching anything (matches this task's own stated figure). Read `analysis/gap_recommendation.py`'s Phase 31 section (`ScenarioCategory`/`recommend_scenario_solutions`/`explain_scenario_candidate`) and Phase 32 section (`RecommendationAudit`, SVG-018 in `docs/BUGS.md`) fully first, plus `analysis/mechanical_archetypes.py`, `analysis/build_archetypes.py`, and `analysis/capability_vector.py` for real available signals -- all three read but, per this phase's own scope, left unmodified.

  Extended `ScenarioCategory` from 4 to 13 values, adding `ANTI_ARMOR`, `ANTI_SHIELD`, `LINE_HOLDING`, `LONG_RANGE_PRESSURE`, `MISSILE_STRIKE`, `PD_SCREEN`, `CARRIER_SUPPORT`, `PURSUIT`, `LOW_COST_REFIT_FRIENDLY` -- 9 of the task's 11 suggested categories. `BREAKTHROUGH` and `ENDURANCE` were deliberately not added: their natural formulas would have reused `ANTI_ARMOR`'s own `ARMOR_BREAKING`/`STRIKER` terms (BREAKTHROUGH) or `LINE_HOLDING`'s `SUSTAINED_PRESSURE`/DEFENSE's own survivability terms (ENDURANCE) at similar weights with no real distinguishing signal available in the current schema -- exactly the "just a relabeled duplicate ranking" outcome the task's own instruction explicitly forbids, documented in `ScenarioCategory`'s own docstring rather than silently omitted.

  Each new category's `_scenario_fit_score` branch cites a real, already-computed signal, most of them newly reused from `analysis/capability_vector.py::CapabilityVector` (not referenced by the original four at all): `ANTI_ARMOR`/`ANTI_SHIELD` from `ARMOR_BREAKING`/`KINETIC_PRESSURE` -- real existing-variant mounted-weapon damage-type fractions, this module's first per-category signal drawn from actual equipped weapons rather than only hull structure/build posture; `LINE_HOLDING` from `SUSTAINED_PRESSURE` (LINE_SHIP structural blended with real flux dissipation -- an output-endurance axis distinct from DEFENSE's survivability-only weighting); `LONG_RANGE_PRESSURE` from `LONG_RANGE_PRESSURE` (ARTILLERY structural blended with real long-range-band mounted-weapon fraction -- the ARTILLERY mechanical archetype was unreferenced by any of the original four); `MISSILE_STRIKE` from `MISSILE_PROJECTION` + `BURST_STRIKE`; `PD_SCREEN` from `PD_SCREENING` + `FIGHTER_INTERCEPTION`, deliberately weighted away from ESCORT's mobility/`SCREEN_ALLIES`-style terms so a stationary heavy PD platform and a fast escort rank distinctly rather than duplicating each other; `CARRIER_SUPPORT` from `CARRIER_PROJECTION` + `FIGHTER_INTERCEPTION` (also previously unreferenced by any of the original four); `PURSUIT` from `PURSUIT` + `MOBILITY` (both fold in verified adapter-derived existing-variant speed effects when available, `analysis/mobility_stats.py`, a strictly stronger real signal than RAIDING's purely qualitative posture check); `LOW_COST_REFIT_FRIENDLY` from `HullFeatureVector.ordnance_points` (inverted, reusing `infer_mechanical_archetypes`'s own 200.0 OP normalization reference rather than inventing a new one) and `.existing_variant_count` -- a genuinely new economic axis no other category references at any weight.

  `_scenario_fit_score` gained an optional `capability: CapabilityVector | None = None` parameter, threaded through both real call sites (`recommend_scenario_solutions`, and `explain_scenario_candidate`'s not-yet-recommended branch) via a new `infer_hull_capability_vector(hull, registry)` call at each; the original four categories (RAIDING/DEFENSE/ESCORT/PATROL) never reference it and are computed byte-identically to before this phase -- verified by the full pre-existing `tests/test_scenario_recommendation.py` suite passing unchanged except the one enum-membership assertion this phase's own category expansion necessarily updates.

  No new heuristic-registry constant or `baseline_0.12` was needed: like the original four, every new category's combination weight is a literal, documented constant inside `_scenario_fit_score` itself (the exact style Phase 31 already established for RAIDING/DEFENSE/ESCORT/PATROL) -- the existing `baseline_0.11` `scenario_fit_min_signal`/`scenario_confidence_cap`/`scenario_recommendation_count` thresholds are reused unchanged for every new category too, so `core/heuristics.py` was not touched at all.

  "Multiple genuinely different best builds per hull": confirmed `recommend_scenario_solutions` already supports this naturally, since it is parameterized by `scenario` and each call independently ranks the same real Native/Retrofit/Acquisition evidence -- there was never a single forced overall ranking to begin with. The real gap was a caller convenience, not a ranking limitation: added `scenario_fits_for_hull(faction, registry, gap_result, hull_id, heuristic_set=..., roles=None, categories=None)`, a pure wrapper that calls the real, unmodified `recommend_scenario_solutions` once per category (default: all 13) and keeps only that hull's own shortlisted entries. It introduces no new ranking/scoring logic and leaves `recommend_scenario_solutions`'s own signature/behavior completely unchanged -- verified directly in a test asserting every entry `scenario_fits_for_hull` returns is `==` the corresponding entry from a direct `recommend_scenario_solutions` call.

  Why-Not consistency: confirmed Phase 32's own `RecommendationAudit` docstring already explains why the scenario leg was deliberately NOT migrated to it -- `explain_scenario_candidate` reuses `recommend_scenario_solutions`'s exact ranking by construction rather than performing an independent ranking of its own, so there was never a separate "ranking vs. Why-Not" computation to drift in the first place. Every prior kind's comparison rule, including `NEGATIVE_EXPECTATION`'s forbidden-set rule, is byte-unchanged (verified by the full pre-existing `tests/test_calibration.py` suite passing unmodified). `analysis/calibration_runner.py` defines `NOT_IN_RANKED_SET = 10**6`, a real, comparable sentinel used whenever an expected candidate never appeared anywhere in the real, bounded set of candidates an observer actually searched -- deliberately a genuine MISMATCH (real evidence against what was actually searched), never a fabricated UNSUPPORTED (which stays reserved for "no ranking data exists at all").

Both hash-bind to the referenced hull's own `source_hash` only (documented limitation: a rescan that changes only the faction's other known hulls, or the scenario category set itself, is not currently detected as stale by that hash alone).   Two real schema facts, found before design, changed the plan from the task's own framing: (1) repo-wide grep confirmed `core/models.py::Weapon` has no typed ammo/ammo-count field at all, and no `.wpn`/`weapon_data.csv` "ammo" column is parsed anywhere in this project -- `MISSILE` mount type is the only real, documented proxy for ammo depletion this schema has (`BALLISTIC`/`ENERGY` weapons draw flux instead), so `ammo_dependence` is implemented and explicitly documented as that mount-type-based proxy, never presented as a real ammo-count read. (2) `core/models.py::Hull` likewise has no typed ship-system field; the real value lives only inside `Hull.raw["ship_data"]["shipSystemId"]` (`.ship` files, `parsers/entities.py::hull_from_row`) or `Hull.raw["skin_data"]["systemId"]` (`.skin` overrides, `hull_from_skin`'s own docstring explicitly lists `systemId` among the fields it preserves verbatim rather than modeling as a typed field) -- `system_complexity` reads those raw keys directly, with the same single-level skin-to-base-hull fallback `_base_mobility_stat`/`_base_defense_stat` already establish for their own CSV-column reads (verified: `_resolve_skins` in `core/scanner.py` only ever resolves a skin against a non-skin base hull, so one level is always sufficient).

  New module `analysis/control_suitability.py`: a frozen `StaticControlSuitability` dataclass with 8 independently-`None`-able, independently `EvidenceClass`-cited signal fields. `range_coherence` (`DIRECT_DATA`): real equipped `Weapon.range` spread/mean, deliberately reading the raw base range rather than `weapon_range_stats.py`'s opt-in, heuristic-set-gated COMBAT-hullmod-adjusted range, so this module carries no `heuristic_set` dependency at all -- a caller wanting the hullmod-adjusted number already has direct access to `weapon_range_stats.py`. `flux_stability` (`DIRECT_DATA`): a raw dissipation/sustained-load ratio, computed the same way `scoring/candidate_score.py::_flux_component` combines `Hull.flux_dissipation`/`Hull.shield_upkeep`/`Weapon.flux_per_second`, but reported as a plain number rather than scored against a heuristic target; `None` only when `sustained_flux_load <= 0` (an undefined ratio, not a fabricated zero). `burst_dependence` (`DIRECT_DATA`): a real seconds-per-shot proxy from `Weapon.flux_per_shot / Weapon.flux_per_second` (Starsector's own "energy/shot"/"energy/second" weapon_data.csv columns), excluding -- never assigning a fabricated interval to -- a weapon with no discrete per-shot cost (e.g. a continuous beam). `ammo_dependence` (`DIRECT_DATA`): the `MISSILE`-mount-type OP-weighted fraction described above; mount counts stay populated even when OP data is incomplete, only the fraction itself goes `None`. `mobility_vs_engagement_range` (`ADAPTER_MODELED`): `analysis/mobility_stats.py::compute_derived_mobility_stats`'s verified effective `max_speed` (falling back to the unmodified base value on a same-stat hullmod-stacking collision, mirroring `scoring/candidate_score.py::_hullmod_adjusted_stat`'s own fallback discipline) reported side by side with real equipped weapon ranges -- deliberately NOT fused into a combined "mismatch score", since no documented Starsector formula establishes a "correct" speed for a given weapon range and inventing one would be exactly the undocumented-behavior guess AGENTS.md forbids. `system_complexity` (`DIRECT_DATA`): real ship-system presence/identity as described above -- a hull whose `ship_data` is present but declares no system is real negative evidence (`has_ship_system=False`), distinct from a hull with no `ship_data`/`skin_data` reachable at all (`None`, genuinely unavailable evidence). `weapon_group_complexity` (`DIRECT_DATA`): real countable equipped-mount count, distinct-mount-type count, and (when the source `.variant` file is available) declared `weaponGroups` count from `Variant.raw["weaponGroups"]` (preserved verbatim by `parsers/entities.py::variant_from_file`) -- a synthetically constructed `Variant` in a test correctly leaves only `weapon_group_count` itself `None` while the two always-derivable mount counts still populate. `survivability_posture` (`ADAPTER_MODELED`): reuses `analysis/combat_stats.py::compute_derived_defense_stats` completely unchanged -- the same verified DEFENSE-hullmod-adjusted armor/hull-HP evidence already backing Phase 12's TANK-archetype mechanical tie-break. No field combines multiple signals into one number, and no aggregate "suitability score" was computed at all, per this phase's own instruction not to invent an unjustified combination weighting.

  Hard framing boundary held throughout, re-read twice before writing any code per the task's own instruction: the module's own docstring states, verbatim, that it is "**NOT a combat outcome predictor**" and never estimates win chance, expected damage traded, time-to-kill, or any other simulated/inferred gameplay outcome -- every field describes a static, structural fact about the build's own already-parsed/adapter-modeled stats, never how it performs. `STATIC_CONTROL_SUITABILITY` appears verbatim in the module docstring and the dataclass's own docstring, matching this phase's exact naming/framing requirement. `ModuleBoundaryTests` (mirroring `tests/test_calibration_activation.py::test_activation_never_touches_the_heuristic_registry`'s own source-text guard pattern) asserts the module's source never references `validation.legality`/`validate_variant`/`LegalityResult`, and never touches `scoring/candidate_score.py`'s ranking machinery either.

Its hull profile report (`_hull_profile_record`, calling `infer_mechanical_archetypes`/`infer_build_archetypes`) is hull/archetype-level -- `BuildArchetypeProfile` describes non-exclusive synthetic build *paths*, not an actual fitted loadout -- so a per-Variant signal set doesn't fit there without restructuring the report shape, which this phase's own instruction says to avoid risking ("if wiring it in risks scope creep... fine to leave standalone... document that choice honestly"). Found a better, lower-risk fit instead: `analysis/variant.py::VariantAnalysis`/`analyze_variant`, a genuine, already-existing per-Variant analyzer aggregator that already calls `compute_derived_mobility_stats`/`compute_derived_defense_stats`/`compute_derived_combat_stats` the same way this new module's own signals are built -- the natural, matching consumer. Added one new, defaulted field, `static_control_suitability: StaticControlSuitability | None = None`, appended at the end of the dataclass (so no existing keyword-argument construction site broke) and computed alongside the other `hull is not None`-gated slices. `cli/main.py`'s `analyze-variant` command already serializes the entire `VariantAnalysis` via a generic `asdict()` call with no field allow-list, so the new signals appear in `svg analyze-variant`'s JSON report with zero CLI code changes. `gui/` was not touched at all, per this phase's hard constraint (confirmed only one construction site of `VariantAnalysis` exists anywhere in `src/`, inside `analyze_variant` itself, by repo-wide grep).

Each of the 8 signals gets both a correctly-computed-value case and a correctly-`None`-on-missing-evidence case: `flux_stability` is `None` when hull dissipation is unknown or any equipped weapon's flux is unknown, and its ratio alone (not the whole signal) is `None` with zero sustained load; `burst_dependence` excludes a beam-style weapon with no per-shot cost from the average without fabricating an interval for it; `ammo_dependence` keeps its mount counts populated even when OP data is unknown; `mobility_vs_engagement_range` proves the base-value fallback and independently proves the "neither known" `None` case; `system_complexity` proves the real ship_data read, the real "no system" negative-evidence case, the skin-override-takes-precedence case, the skin-falls-back-to-base-hull case, and the fully-unavailable `None` case; `weapon_group_complexity` proves real group/mount-type counting from a realistic `.variant`-shaped `raw["weaponGroups"]` payload and the partial-`None` case; `survivability_posture` proves it reuses `compute_derived_defense_stats`'s real `reinforcedhull` +40% hull-HP effect unchanged.   None were genuine runtime bugs. Every error traced to one of three honestly-stale-annotation causes, verified against the real construction/call sites before concluding so: (1) `RecommendationAudit.rank`/`own_score` were typed `int | None`/`float | None`, but a grep of all 6 real `RecommendationAudit(...)` construction sites (native/retrofit/acquisition x build-aware/legacy) confirmed both are *always* supplied a real value (`index + 1`, and a real computed score) -- narrowed both fields to plain `int`/`float`. (2) `_best_retrofit_for_hull` had no return-type annotation, which mypy resolves to `Any` at call sites (confirmed with a standalone repro) -- explaining why its build-aware caller's `result.after_score`/`.total_change_cost` usage produced no errors while the legacy caller's own explicit (and wrong) `object`-typed list annotation for the same value did. Added a proper `_RetrofitOpportunity` return dataclass with already-narrowed `before_score`/`after_score` floats (the two fields `_best_retrofit_for_hull`'s own loop already confirms non-`None` before ever returning a candidate), replacing the untyped tuple and the incorrect `object` annotation in both callers. (3) `_native_audit_trail`/`_retrofit_audit_trail`/`_acquisition_audit_trail` each reused the same local names (`ranked`/`selected`/`selection_reasons`/`selection_order`) for both their build-aware and legacy branches despite genuinely different tuple/dict-key shapes in each -- mutually exclusive at runtime (never actually queried with the wrong shape) but unified into one incompatible static type by mypy across the `if`/`else`. Renamed the legacy branch's locals (`ranked_hulls`, `selected_hulls`, `hull_selection_reasons`, `hull_selection_order`) in all three functions rather than adding casts. Also gave `_scenario_fit_score`'s incrementally-extended `evidence` accumulator an explicit `tuple[str, ...]` annotation (each scenario branch legitimately appends 1-2 more strings onto the same name).

  One near-miss reviewed carefully per this task's own instruction to treat `Callable[[RecommendationAudit], int | None]` sort/min keys as a real crash-risk candidate, not a style nit: `audit.selection_order` (genuinely `int | None` -- only set for `recommended=True` entries) was being sorted/read directly at `recommend_native_solutions`/`recommend_retrofit_solutions`/`recommend_acquisition_solutions`'s three shortlist-assembly sites. Traced the invariant by hand across all 6 audit-trail construction sites: `selection_reasons`/`selection_order` are always built from the identical `selected` shortlist within the same statement, so `recommended=True` and `selection_order is not None` can never actually diverge -- confirmed genuinely unreachable, not a live bug. Still added `_require_selection_order(audit) -> int`, a named, documented type-narrowing accessor that raises a clear `ValueError` (naming the hull/leg/role) instead of leaving a bare `TypeError` risk in place or silencing it with a cast -- real defense-in-depth for an invariant that is airtight today but not mechanically enforced by the type system, per this task's explicit "give a real, documented fallback" instruction for this exact error shape. Added `RecommendationAuditSelectionOrderInvariantTests` (`tests/test_gap_recommendation.py`, 3 new tests) constructing `RecommendationAudit` directly to prove the accessor is correct on the real path and fails loudly, not silently or cryptically, if the invariant were ever violated.

  Root-caused the largest single pattern (7 errors: `classify_weapon`/`classify_civilian_role`/`recommendation_eligibility`/`infer_combat_doctrine`/`classify_fighter`/`classify_fighter_wing_entity`/`classify_hullmod` all called with `Entity` where a specific subtype was required) to `core/registry.py::Registry`, whose `hulls`/`weapons`/`fighters`/`hullmods`/`variants`/`factions` fields were declared as bare, unparametrized `EntityIndex` (mypy widens this to `EntityIndex[Any]`) even though `Registry.from_scan` always builds each one from its correspondingly-typed `ScanResult` list (`scan.hulls: list[Hull]`, etc.) -- a real, always-homogeneous collection with a lost static type, not a genuinely mixed one, confirmed by reading `from_scan` and `ScanResult`'s own field types before concluding so. Parametrized all 6 fields (`EntityIndex[Hull]`, `EntityIndex[Weapon]`, ...) and corrected `weapons_matching`/`hulls_matching`/`fighters_matching`/`hullmods_matching`'s return types from `tuple[Entity, ...]` to their real per-entity type -- fixing the gap at its source rather than casting at each of `api.py`'s call sites.

  Fixed the second dominant pattern (`**dict[str, ...]` splat calls into `build_archetype_candidates_fingerprint`/`generate_build_archetype_candidates`/`candidate_alternatives_fingerprint`/`generate_candidate_alternatives`, all inside `api.py::run_generate`) by restructuring all 6 call sites from a shared `dict(...)` splat to explicit named keyword arguments -- the values were always exactly what each callee's parameters expect, but mypy cannot verify a runtime dict-splat's inferred value-type union against each keyword's own declared type; explicit keywords carry the identical values and are checked per-argument. No narrowly-scoped `type: ignore` was needed for either dominant pattern.

  While fixing `run_generate`'s knock-on `assessed_candidates`/`candidates` indexing and assignment errors, found a real, if currently inert, type-shape inconsistency: `GenerateOutcome.candidates` (declared `list[CandidateResult]`) was assigned a genuine `list` in the build-archetype branch but a genuine `tuple` (`generate_candidate_alternatives`'s and `candidate_alternatives_result_from_payload`'s real return type) in the plain-profile branch -- the same field held different real container types depending on which branch ran. Confirmed by repo-wide grep that no caller does list-specific mutation or an `isinstance` check on it (only `len()`/indexing/JSON serialization, which behave identically for both), so this was never externally observable and did not warrant its own `docs/BUGS.md` entry -- normalized both branches to always build a real `list` via `list(...)`, matching the already-declared field type, with zero JSON/CLI-observable change. Also fixed `run_query_faction_equipment`'s declared return type (`-> list`, but its body always returns `Registry.faction_equipment`'s real `dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]`) and, to avoid rippling a new `cli/main.py` error into the `query` command's shared `records` variable, gave `records` an explicit `list[dict] | dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]` annotation documenting the real union all 6 `query` sub-entities produce.

  Found and fixed a genuine, previously-undetected runtime bug while investigating why `cli/main.py`'s `why-not` command's `result`-equivalent local kept getting reused across command branches with incompatible real types (`api.run_why_not` actually returns `CombinedWhyNotExplanation | BuildWhyNotExplanation`, a real union): the command's print block unconditionally accessed `explanation.native.reason`/`.retrofit.reason`/`.acquisition.reason`, which only exist on `CombinedWhyNotExplanation`. Every real `why-not --build-archetype <id>` invocation (a documented flag, `GAP_RECOMMENDATION_ENGINE.md` section 13) raised `AttributeError` instead of printing a report, and no existing test exercised `cli/main.py`'s command handling at all (only `api.run_why_not`/`explain_build_candidate` directly) to have caught it. Filed as `docs/BUGS.md` SVG-019. Fixed by extracting the print logic into a new, directly testable `_why_not_report_lines(explanation)` that `isinstance`-narrows on `BuildWhyNotExplanation`; the `CombinedWhyNotExplanation` path prints byte-identical output to before. New `tests/test_cli_main.py` (2 tests, synthetic dataclass fixtures for both explanation shapes, no scan/registry needed) covers both.

  The remaining ~80 `cli/main.py` errors were all one root pattern: roughly a dozen bare, unannotated locals (`profile`, `constraints`, `result`, `outcome`, ...) reused across `main()`'s ~16 independent, mutually-exclusive `if args.command == "...": ... return` command branches, each holding a genuinely different real result/constraint type per branch (e.g. the generic `result` name held `GapRecommendationResult` in `recommend`, then `FleetSupportResult` in `fleet-support`, then `FleetSupportWhyNotExplanation`, then `ScenarioFleetAssessment`, then `RefitResult`, then `QualityRefitResult`). mypy infers an unannotated local's declared type from its first assignment in the function and checks every later assignment against it, even across provably-disjoint early-return branches, so every later branch's reuse of the same bare name was flagged as an incompatible assignment, cascading into "no such attribute" errors on the wrongly-inferred type. Fixed by giving each command branch its own uniquely-named local (`capability_profile`, `support_constraints`, `fleet_result`, `fleet_why_not_result`, `scenario_result`, `fix_result`, `quality_result`, `scan_outcome`, ...) -- a pure rename with no behavior change, verified by the unchanged full-suite pass and by every branch's own JSON/print output referencing only its own now-correctly-typed local.

  Recurring root causes, found across most files rather than one-off: (1) a dict/tuple literal combining several differently-parametrized instances of an invariant generic (`EntityIndex[Hull]`, `EntityIndex[Weapon]`, ...) collapses to `object` -- fixed with an explicit `dict[str, EntityIndex[Any]]`/`tuple[Entity, ...]`-shaped annotation at each of 4 occurrences (`core/registry.py` x2, `core/knowledge_packs.py` x2, `analysis/mod_acceptance.py`), matching the precedent `core/registry.py`'s own `Registry` dataclass fields already established in the concurrent `api.py`/`cli/main.py` pass. (2) A local variable name reused for a genuinely different type later in the same function scope (mypy fixes a local's type from its first assignment, including loop variables, which don't get their own scope in Python) -- found and fixed by renaming at 5 sites (`generation/candidate.py::_build_candidate`'s `weapon`, `analysis/mod_acceptance.py`'s `hull`, `gui/main_window.py`'s `widget`, plus two more folded into other fixes), never by adding a cast. (3) A tuple literal's length fixes its static arity (`tuple[str]` from a 1-element literal) even though the variable is later reassigned a longer tuple -- fixed with an explicit `tuple[str, ...]` annotation at the point of first assignment (`analysis/capability_vector.py`, `gui/resources.py`). (4) A helper's own documented contract ("only returns `None` when `base_value` is `None`") is real but not mechanically provable to the type checker at a call site where `base_value` was already null-checked earlier -- fixed with a named intermediate variable plus a comment-justified `assert`, never a bare ignore (`generation/vent_cap.py`, `scoring/candidate_score.py`, both mirroring the exact same `_hullmod_adjusted_*` pattern).

  Two real, if narrow, latent bugs were found and fixed (both filed in `docs/BUGS.md`, next available IDs after the concurrent `api.py`/`cli/main.py` pass's SVG-019): **SVG-020** (`analysis/mechanical_archetypes.py::infer_hull_feature_vector`) -- `variants` and `registry` are independently optional parameters, but the per-variant weapon-evidence loop unconditionally dereferenced `registry.weapons.by_id` whenever a variant had weapons, with no `registry is not None` guard; a caller supplying `variants` explicitly while omitting `registry` (no real caller does today, confirmed by auditing every call site in `src/`/`tests/`) would crash with `AttributeError` instead of gracefully omitting weapon-level evidence the same way a no-weapons variant already does. Fixed by adding the missing guard; fighter-wing evidence (needs no registry) is unaffected. **SVG-021** (`gui/main_window.py::_populate_missing_retrofits`) -- the background `operation` lambda read `self._current_hull.id` directly instead of capturing `hull_id = self._current_hull.id` first, unlike every sibling background operation in this file (`_generate`, `_refit`, faction analysis, export), which capture exactly this way specifically because `_run`'s operation only executes once its worker thread starts, after the click. A user changing the hull selection in that window could generate a retrofit for the wrong hull or crash the worker on a cleared selection. The same call's `completed` callback was also a `lambda result: (self.refit_detail.setPlainText(...), self._refresh_editable_retrofit_library())` -- a tuple-literal "two statements in one lambda" trick that happened to run both side effects correctly today but was untestable in isolation and built/discarded a throwaway `(None, None)`; extracted into a real, directly testable, properly `-> None` method, `_apply_populate_missing_retrofits_result`. 3 new regression tests total (1 in `tests/test_mechanical_archetypes.py`, 2 in `tests/test_gui_canvas.py`, the second pair exercising the exact race via a captured `operation` callable rather than a real `QThread` -- background-thread completion timing isn't reliably observable in this headless harness, per the existing `test_apply_incremental_scan_outcome_...` test's own documented reasoning).

- 2026-08-31 (mypy sweep closeout, `analysis/fleet_support.py`): the three scoped passes above deliberately excluded this file since it is a concurrent process's own new module with no available design context; a final whole-`src` `mypy` run afterward showed it held all 19 remaining errors in the codebase (241 total at the start of this whole effort). Given the user's request to fix all 241 without carve-outs, and that several of these errors looked like real `None`-handling gaps rather than pure annotation noise, read the file in full before touching it (single read, ~700 lines) rather than delegating blindly.

  Root causes, all behavior-preserving except one real bug: (1) `_mean(values, default=0.0)` is typed `-> float | None` even when a non-`None` default is supplied (mypy cannot special-case by argument value), so 4 call sites (`_support_needs`'s `confidence`, `_cohesion`, both `_defensive_match` locals) needed the same `or 0.0` idiom the file already uses everywhere else it calls `_mean` with a default -- applied for consistency, not invented fresh. (2) `_support_needs`'s `names` local was first assigned `_LOGISTICS_NEEDS` (a 4-tuple), fixing its inferred type at that literal arity; later branches assigning differently-sized tuples then failed -- fixed with an explicit `names: tuple[str, ...]` annotation. (3) `_composition_traits` reused a `values` local for two structurally different list comprehensions across two separate `for` loops in the same function scope (a `list[CapabilityEvidence]` first, a `list[float]` second) -- renamed the second one to `axis_values`, matching this exact reused-local-name pattern already fixed at 5 other sites across the codebase this session. (4) `_variant_weapon_range`/`_selection_weapon_range` filtered `registry.weapons.by_id[weapon_id].range is not None` in a comprehension's `if` clause but read the same expression again as the yielded value -- mypy does not narrow through repeated dict-subscript expressions, only through a bound name -- fixed with a walrus-bound `weapon`/`weapon_range` pair, mirroring the file's own established `if (hull := _selection_hull(...)) is not None` idiom. (5) `_score_candidate`/`_composition_synergy` declared their `values` parameter as `dict[str, float]`, but every real caller passes `get_heuristic_set(...).values`, which is a read-only `Mapping[str, float]`; both parameters are only ever read via `.get(...)`, never mutated, so widened to `Mapping[str, float]` (added `from collections.abc import Mapping`) rather than forcing a pointless `dict(...)` copy at the call site. (6) `fleet_support_request_from_payload` iterated `payload.get("selections", [])` directly, where `payload: dict[str, object]` makes that call's result type `object` (not iterable) -- bound it to `raw_selections` first and guarded with `isinstance(raw_selections, list)`.

  One real bug, filed as **SVG-022**: `analyze_player_fleet`'s structurally-ineligible branch appended `selection.hull_id` to a `list[str]` -- for a variant-only selection (`FleetSelection(variant_id=...)`, `hull_id` unset) that resolves to a structurally ineligible hull, this appended `None` into a tuple documented and typed as `tuple[str, ...]`, silently corrupting `PlayerFleetProfile.excluded_selection_hull_ids` and the GUI's "Structurally excluded selections" line for that case. The same function already computes `label = selection.variant_id or selection.hull_id or "<empty>"` a few lines above for exactly this reason; fixed by appending `label` instead. New regression test `tests/test_fleet_support.py::test_structurally_ineligible_variant_selection_is_recorded_by_its_own_label_not_none` builds a variant-only selection resolving to a `FIGHTER`-sized hull and asserts the recorded id is the variant's own, not `None`.
