- 2026-08-31 (Ruff findings review, all 222): reviewed every non-cosmetic finding by hand before fixing anything in bulk, since several categories looked like they could hide real bugs rather than pure style noise.

  Manually reviewed and fixed with judgment, not blind autofix: **SVG-023** (new, filed **Open**) -- `analysis/build_archetypes.py::infer_build_archetypes`'s `player_suitability` field was `"GOOD" if style == "FLANK_AND_COMMIT" else "GOOD"`, a dead ternary (`RUF034`) that always evaluates to `"GOOD"` regardless of build style or maturity, so the field currently carries zero information. Its sibling `ai_suitability` field one line above has a real, sensible gate (`"CONDITIONAL"` for non-`VIABLE` maturity or `FLANK_AND_COMMIT` style, matching real Starsector AI's documented difficulty with aggressive timed-flank builds) -- `player_suitability` looks like an incomplete copy-paste of that logic. Given this project's own established caution around AI/player-suitability framing (`ROADMAP.md`'s `STATIC_CONTROL_SUITABILITY`, deliberately never "AI combat prediction"), did not guess at the intended replacement logic; collapsed the dead ternary to the literal `"GOOD"` it already always produced (closes the lint finding, zero behavior change) and filed the real semantic gap as Open with a plausible fix shape for a human to decide. Two loop-variable closures flagged by `B023` (`analysis/gap_recommendation.py`'s `diversity_key`, `analysis/fleet_support.py`'s `key`, both inside a `while` loop reassigning the captured list each iteration) were confirmed *not* currently live bugs -- each closure is defined and consumed via `max(pool, key=...)` synchronously within the same iteration, before the next iteration's reassignment -- but were still hardened with the standard `selected_ids: list[str] = selected_ids` default-argument early-bind idiom, since a future refactor that stored or deferred either closure would silently break otherwise, and this is exactly the closure-capture bug class this session already hit twice for real (SVG-021 and its own kind). `PLW0127` in `generation/hullmods.py`'s `select_hullmods` (`allowed_hullmod_ids = allowed_hullmod_ids`, a genuine no-op) was confirmed dead -- the parameter's real handling (`is not None` check further down) doesn't need any normalization -- and deleted outright, unlike the adjacent `denied_hullmod_ids`/`preferred_hullmod_ids` lines which do real `or set()` defaulting and were left untouched.

  `TRY004` (12 occurrences, "prefer `TypeError` for a failed `isinstance` check") was deliberately **not** applied anywhere, after checking real catch sites first: `core/scanner.py`'s cache-snapshot loader already catches `(..., ValueError, ..., TypeError)` together so it would have been safe there, but `analysis/fleet_support.py`, `analysis/scenario_advisor.py`, `analysis/video_review.py`, `profiles/advanced.py`, and `parsers/common.py::json_file` all raise `ValueError` for a malformed externally-sourced document (a saved request snapshot, an advanced-request JSON file, a video-review transcript, a raw mod source file) as a deliberate, consistent, codebase-wide convention -- confirmed `gui/main_window.py` catches `ValueError` specifically around `fleet_support_request_from_payload`/`scenario_advisor_request_from_payload`/`parse_fleet_selections` to show a friendly error dialog (lines ~1425-1546), and confirmed `core/scanner.py`'s own per-source-file try blocks are *not* uniformly broad (some, e.g. lines 115/159, catch `(OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError)` without `TypeError`) -- so applying Ruff's generic heuristic anywhere in this set would have silently turned a caught, user-facing "malformed file" error into an uncaught crash. `B008` (10 occurrences, "no function call in a default argument") was also left alone everywhere: every instance defaults to either a `@dataclass(frozen=True)` construction (`FleetSupportConstraints()`) or a Qt value type (`QModelIndex()`), both safe-by-construction against the classic mutable-default-argument hazard this rule exists to catch, and restructuring 8+ call sites to a `None`-sentinel pattern would add boilerplate for no real benefit.

  Every other real-looking finding was individually traced before deciding it was fine as-is or applying a small, targeted fix (never a blind rename): `F841`/`RUF059` unused-variable findings in `cli/main.py` (`list_profiles = subparsers.add_parser(...)`, the assignment was pointless -- this subcommand needs no further `.add_argument` calls, unlike every other captured subparser in the file), `generation/candidate.py` (`profile = get_profile(profile_id)`, confirmed intentional fail-fast validation -- `_build_candidate` re-resolves the same profile internally two lines later, so the caller's copy was genuinely unneeded, not a dropped use), `analysis/fleet_support.py::explain_fleet_support_candidate` (`profile` from `_rank_fleet_support`'s 3-tuple, truly unused), `tests/test_gui_canvas.py` (an unused `operation` unpack and an unused `TemporaryDirectory(...) as tmp` -- confirmed the temp dir itself is never touched by that specific test, unlike 16 sibling tests in the same file that do use theirs), and `tests/test_vent_cap.py` (`registry` unpacked but deliberately unused -- the test's whole point, per its own comment, is verifying `registry=None` behavior). Two `ISC004` implicit-string-concatenation findings in `analysis/gap_recommendation.py` were confirmed to be legitimate intentional multi-line f-string joins forming one tuple element each (not a missing comma), just needing explicit grouping parens. `C401`/`RUF012`/`PLC0206` in test files (`test_gap_recommendation.py`, `test_scenario_recommendation.py`) were mechanical, zero-risk rewrites (generator -> set comprehension, a `ClassVar` annotation on a read-only test fixture dict, `.items()` instead of re-indexing by key).

  After all of the above, ran `ruff check --fix` (safe fixes only, no `--unsafe-fixes`) for the remaining bulk-cosmetic majority: 125 `I001` unsorted-imports, 19 `UP035` deprecated `typing` imports, 13 `UP037` unnecessary quoted annotations, 10 `F401` unused imports, 4 `PYI041` redundant numeric unions, 4 `RUF100` unused `noqa` comments, plus one each of `UP017`/`SIM114`/`RET501`/`B009`x2 -- 200 fixed automatically. This surfaced one real regression, caught immediately by re-running `mypy` rather than assuming a lint-only tool couldn't break typing: `output/analysis_reports.py`'s import-sorting fix landed on top of an in-progress edit already on disk from the concurrent process sharing this repo (their own `object` -> `Hull`/`Entity`/`Sequence[Entity]` type-tightening pass through this same file, evidently mid-flight -- confirmed via `git diff`, not reverted), which had tightened several function signatures but left one helper, `_entity_sort_key(entity: object)`, still `object`-typed; once its 4 real callers were more precisely typed, mypy could see `entity.source_mod`/`entity.id` weren't provable on a bare `object`. Fixed by widening its parameter to `Entity` -- the same base class the concurrent process's own edit had already imported into this file for exactly this purpose, and the type every one of its 4 real call sites already passes.

  Final Ruff count: 222 -> 22 (12 `TRY004` + 10 `B008`, both deliberately left with the reasoning above -- not unreviewed). `mypy src/` re-confirmed clean (0 errors) after the `analysis_reports.py` fix. Full suite: 764 passing, 1 skipped, 0 regressions. `from starsector_variant_generator.gui.main_window import MainWindow` re-confirmed importable after the import-sorting pass touched `gui/` files. New `docs/BUGS.md` entry: SVG-023 (Open, awaiting a human decision on `player_suitability` semantics).

## Traceability rules

- Every scan records source errors, warnings, and heuristic provenance.
- Every observed defect receives an ID in `BUGS.md`.
- Generated inventories are derived from the scan report; source files remain untouched.
- Later phases must add an entry here with tests run and report locations.
